## ★ B-DISCORD open items (2026-06-20)

> Source: `Scope Files/B_DISCORD_SCOPE.md` + punch list `B_DISCORD_FOLLOWUPS_2026-06-20.md`. The Discord comms fabric is built + parallel + unswitched (`COMMS_BACKEND=telegram`).

End-to-end test PASSED: staging `fire-due` fired a warning alert → "Discord alert posted" → channel post as "DawnTrader Alerts" → Langston bridge `ALERT enqueued ... via alerts webhook` (always-engaged, bypassed the name-gate) → triaged correctly → alert resolved. B-DISCORD CLOSED. _(History — prior state:)_ Design Langston-approved + now BUILT: (bridge) `discord-langston-bridge.py` always-engages when `message.webhook_id == CFG.alerts_webhook_id` (bypasses the start-with-"Langston" gate + circuit breaker, alert-triage prompt, never [SILENT], name-prepend skipped) + `discord_common.load_shared_config` reads `ALERTS_WEBHOOK_ID` — deployed + verified inert (`alerts_webhook_id=None`); (staging) `scripts/system-alerts.ts` `fire-due` now calls `pushToDiscord(alert)` alongside `pushToTelegram` — direct HTTPS POST to the secret webhook URL (read from `/etc/langston/discord-alerts-webhook.env` or env), no-op when absent, warning+critical gating mirrors Telegram (commit `e4b5499be`, tsc baseline clean). Both sides are NO-OPs until provisioned. **BLOCKER (activation only):** Kyle must create ONE #general webhook ("DawnTrader Alerts") and hand CC the URL (→ staging secret file) + ID (→ bridge config `ALERTS_WEBHOOK_ID` in `/etc/dawntrader/discord-comms.env`) — CC cannot create webhooks. Then: live-test one alert. **HOME:** B-DISCORD; batch stays OPEN until activated+verified. **OPEN (code-complete; blocked-on-Kyle for activation).** *(NOTE: CI is red on the branch from reorg-B2 Step-3's `telemetry_history.source` migration drift — NEW Claude's batch, NOT this change; OBJ-5's tsc gate is clean.)*
- **#333 — Discord cutover (flip `COMMS_BACKEND=telegram`→`discord`). IMMINENT (Kyle 2026-06-21 considers Discord PROVEN).** Plan: ONE more batch from CC-A (B-GOV-3) + ONE more from CC-B (reorg-B2.1) — **★ over Discord ONLY, ZERO Telegram (Kyle 2026-06-21, hardened from "optional"): test Discord IN ISOLATION.** Kyle's reason: while the Telegram bridges still run, a CC/Langston could be getting woken/informed via Telegram and THEN responding on Discord — making Discord look more standalone-functional than it is. The deep CLAUDE.md §6.5-strip is ON HOLD (the R3 slim is in doubt — see #339). **HOME:** Kyle-gated cutover after the two batches. **OPEN (imminent).**
- **#340 — System-alert HANDLING PROTOCOL (Kyle directive 2026-06-21).** _(Renumbered from #338 → #340 2026-06-21 to deconflict with CC-B's reorg-B2.1 #338 — the per-session number-collision hazard; see MEMORY number-reservation convention.)_ Today Langston receives an alert, diagnoses it, sometimes farms it to a CC — but sometimes "nothing comes back" (alerts dropped via Telegram-era build gaps / the circuit-breaker / the relay issue). Relates to §10.5 + the new Discord alerts feed + the `langston-alert-invokes.log` "invoke DONE" CC-wake (the existing partial mechanism) + the 2026-06-11 "Langston ACK ≠ resolved" gap-fix. **HOME: its own near-future batch (e.g. B-ALERT-PROTOCOL); can come after the CLAUDE.md-file question (#339) is settled.** **OPEN.**

The deep Telegram §6.5-strip folded into the slim rides with that decision. **OPEN (queued; trim-vs-keep decided at the batch).**

| 105 | ✅ RESOLVED 2026-05-13 (BATCH_80) — TEC re-keyed by tradeId. Engine state per-trade. Option C+ rehydrate seed preserves in-flight `tradeMode` + `ladderRung` + `originalStopPrice` across restarts with engine-side defensive coercion for null-rung TRAILING_TAKE seeds. Runtime invariant `[B80][TEC_KEYING_INVARIANT_VIOLATION]` on every exit-cycle. 10 unit tests passing. Affects VTS + paper + live exit cycles. UI category line added in Open/Closed Simulated Trades per Kyle directive. PM2 #269 deployed; staging monitoring confirms zero invariant violations, multi-trade-per-symbol cases resolve correctly (XRP/GBP visibly has 2 independent trades with separate tradeIds + stops). See `Claude Comms and Packages/Batch Completion/BATCH_80_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`. **Original issue:** **TEC `trailingStates` keyed by SYMBOL instead of trade.id — concurrent trades on the same symbol share one engine state.** `server/services/trailing-exit-controller.ts` stores `trailingStates: Map<string, TrailingState>` keyed by symbol (lines 601, 613, 868, 870, 1098, 1108, 1172). VTS + paper + live trading paths all call `evaluateTECExit`/`updateTrailingState` and share the SAME map. **Multiple concurrent trades on the same symbol** (different strategies / different lanes — e.g., FET/USD with range_trade + support_bounce + morning_star open simultaneously per 2026-05-13 CSV export) **share ONE TEC state**. First trade to call `initializeTrailingState` sets the shared `currentStopPrice` + `originalStopPrice` + `highWaterMark`; subsequent trades inherit those values and the engine evaluates ALL of them against the FIRST trade's stop. When any one trade closes, `clearTrailingState(symbol)` (vts-runner.ts:2500, paper-execution-engine.ts:1352) wipes the shared state — next exit-cycle re-initializes from whichever surviving trade iterates first, so remaining trades on the symbol now follow a totally different trade's geometry. **Real-world impact:** displayed `trade.stopLoss` and engine `currentStopPrice` diverge for any trade after the first one on a given symbol. UI shows the trade's own per-trade stop; engine evaluates against the shared state's stop. Trades stay alive past their displayed stops (FET/USD range_trade in 2026-05-13 CSV: displayed stop 0.22863, engine stop 0.2117, current 0.225 → below displayed but above engine → still alive after 58h). BE-latch / target-latch / moonbag fires on one trade's geometry but the new floor applies to ALL concurrent trades on the symbol. Trailing HWM is conflated across trades. **Crypto duplicate-guard caps at 1 per (symbol, strategy) but allows different strategies → bug manifests in `quant-strong_trend` lane (vwap_pullback + strong_bull_trend co-eligible) and across regime transitions.** Active trading paper/live have no per-symbol cap → bug applies equally. **Fix proposal (B-NEW-24 / BATCH_80):** key `trailingStates` by trade.id (or positionId for paper/live). Plumb tradeId through `TECUpdate`. Update all reads + writes (getTrailingState, clearTrailingState, persistence migration). Recommend collapsing `trade.stopLoss` and `engine.currentStopPrice` into a single source of truth (UI Option C: display always = engine trigger). Needs full workflow with Langston design review BEFORE implementation per NO PATCHES doctrine (CLAUDE.md §5 #15). Bundled in BATCH_80 with the open-trades UI label change (crypto/xstock category line between symbol and asset class). | OPEN — needs design review + dedicated batch (BATCH_80) | Surfaced 2026-05-13 from FET/USD CSV export. Affects VTS + paper + live exit cycles. Root-cause analysis committed in tracker; design ask drafted in `Claude Comms and Packages/Langston Design Asks/B80_TEC_per_trade_keying_design_ask_rev1.md`. |

| 116 | ✅ **PARTIALLY RESOLVED 2026-05-20 (B-NEW-36 sub-batch (b)) — xstock_spot fail-closed noise eliminated by side-effect; crypto_perp / xstock_perp still open.** Off-hours session-lifecycle controller now bulk-marks all open xStock VTS trades as `state='weekend_suspended'` during the Fri 8PM ET → Sun 8PM ET unified weekend close window. The VTS sim cycle `resolveOpenVirtualTrades` iteration filter (`if (t.state === 'weekend_suspended') continue;`) skips these trades, which means TEC `resolveTECConfig()` is NOT called for xstock_spot during the weekend window — eliminating the fail-closed log noise that was the primary observable failure. The underlying refresh-on-demand architecture remains; xstock_spot during ARCA hours, crypto_perp, and xstock_perp still rely on sporadic-consumer-driven refresh. If those classes' fail-closed noise becomes problematic, a separate background-timer batch can be opened (sister to #109's "stale-with-no-consumer" framing). Verification gate: zero `TEC_STALE_FAIL_CLOSED` events for asset_class=xstock_spot expected in the Fri 2026-05-22 8 PM ET → Sun 2026-05-24 8 PM ET window. Original entry: **TEC refresh-on-demand misses sporadic-consumer asset classes — fail-closed correctly fires but generates noise (extends #109).** Surfaced 2026-05-18 00:15 UTC ~2 hours after B-PHASE-A2 deploy (PM2 #294). `/api/diagnostics/tec-config` shows: crypto_spot HEALTHY (refreshed every ~60s, consumer = FX5 scanner cycle), but crypto_perp / xstock_spot / xstock_perp ALL `staleByCeiling: true`, lastSuccessAt = deploy time. **Root cause:** TEC config refresh is on-demand (triggered by `resolveTECConfig()` calls), not via independent background timer. Classes with continuous-rate callers (crypto_spot via FX5 scanner) stay fresh. Classes with sporadic callers (xstock_spot only via VTS sim cycle hitting open xStock virtual trades AND/OR xstock scanner runCycle when ARCA-open) go stale during quiet periods. Fence fires from `runPhase10SimulationCycle → resolveOpenVirtualTrades → evaluateTECExit → isMoonbagQualifier → resolveTECConfig` stack. **Operationally safe** — fence is the protection mechanism, working as designed (refuses to make decisions on stale config); zero money at risk (VTS sim is learning-only). NOT a B-PHASE-A2 regression (refresh-on-demand pattern predates A.2; same scenario would have fired on B-NEW-42b PM2 #293 had a VTS sim cycle hit it then). **Fix shape:** small batch — add a background timer in `tec-config-cache.ts` (or equivalent) that calls `primeTECConfig()` for all 4 active asset classes every 4 min regardless of consumer activity. Sister of #109's "stale-with-no-consumer" framing → this is "stale-with-sporadic-consumer where the fence actively fires." Until ship: each fire is a single log line; next deploy resets the cache; Mon ARCA-open xStock scanner activity will auto-refresh the xstock_spot cache (sustained-rate consumer). | PARTIALLY RESOLVED — B-NEW-36 (b) eliminates xstock_spot weekend instance; crypto_perp / xstock_perp residual still open | Surfaced 2026-05-18 00:15 UTC as live fail-closed event post-B-PHASE-A2 deploy. xstock_spot weekend instance closed via B-NEW-36 sub-batch (b) 2026-05-20. |
| 123 | **External macro feed (`external-macro-feed.ts`) is asset-class-agnostic.** Surfaced 2026-05-20 PM during B79.0n umbrella v1 "prove-me-wrong" audit (Langston item 13b). MCE has a "macro modifier" but the upstream macro feed itself doesn't differentiate per asset class. Crypto-relevant macro signals (BTC dominance, ETF flows, funding resets) are structurally different from xStock-relevant macro signals (rates, earnings calendar, macro econ releases). Deferred from B79.0n umbrella as a post-arc B79.x follow-up batch. (c) The wider TS2339 cluster across active-trading services (autonomy, ethics, reasoning, knowledge, memory, drift, learning, validation, paper-execution, signal-orchestrator, ready-to-buy, vts-runner, unified-core, etc.). **Phase 19 disposition path:** piece-by-piece active-trading-path walkthrough — for each cluster decide (1) does the feature stay in the current architecture? (2) is the wiring superseded? (3) populate the missing fields vs. replace the mechanism vs. delete. Per-file structured intake (consumer/producer locations, current execution state) lives in the baseline file's per-file `context` strings. **Resequencing decision (Kyle approved 2026-05-23 on CC + Langston joint advisory):** Phase 19 runs BEFORE Phase 16 legacy-code cleanup — restoration creates ground truth on dead-vs-dormant; cleanup acts on that ground truth. Infra-clean parts of Phase 16 (DB schema rationalization / column drops independent of active-trading code surface) can still front-run safely. **POST_AUDIT_ROADMAP updated at B-NEW-43 Phase 1 close (2026-05-23) — see "2026-05-23 update — Phase 19 runs BEFORE Phase 16" block.** **Identified Phase-19 follow-ups (logged during B-NEW-43 implementation):**
- **Cost-telemetry regime-tag misleadingness (B-NEW-43 chunk 11, 2026-05-23).** `server/core/telemetry/cost-telemetry.ts:99` inserts cost-telemetry rows with `regime: REGIMES.TREND_FRIENDLY_STABLE` as a placeholder (the predecessor name `BULL_STABLE` was renamed in the canonical-regime overhaul and chunk 11 updated the reference to match the live enum). Cost-telemetry itself is NOT regime-conditional — it tracks costs across all regimes — but the schema requires a regime label, so every row gets tagged TREND_FRIENDLY_STABLE. Any future cost-by-regime query reads as "all costs occurred in TREND_FRIENDLY_STABLE" — analytically misleading. **Phase 19 should decide the right answer:** a dedicated sentinel regime value (`ALL` / `N_A`), making the column nullable, or splitting cost-telemetry into a non-regime-keyed table. Flagged 2026-05-23 by Langston in the chunk-11 Step 4 ACK.

Phase 19 needs to know which baselined sites have schema-drift fingerprints vs. pure semantic-drift: (i) `server/services/fx5-scanner.ts:1453` — `familyPaths` field written to `ScanDiagnostics` not on the type. (iii) `server/storage.ts:4201` — `stoppedBy` field written to a stop-event log; schema doesn't have the column. **Phase 19 disposition path for these specifically:** confirm whether the table is currently the canonical persistence layer for the conceptual field (in which case the schema gets the column added) or whether the field is dormant code-side (in which case the code reference gets removed). | 140 | **Deploy procedure refinement — apply migration BEFORE pm2 restart to eliminate fail-hard WARN window (Langston B79.0n.CONFIDENCE-CHAIN Step 8 observation, 2026-05-25).** During the B79.0n.CONFIDENCE-CHAIN Step 6 staging deploy at 17:51-17:55 UTC, the deploy procedure was: (1) git pull, (2) npm run db:migrate, (3) npm run build, (4) pm2 restart dawntrader. The migration step completed correctly but the live PM2 process was still running with the OLD code that didn't expect the new `b67_1_asset_class_no_op_active` constant. The MCE refresh cycle ticked every minute during the deploy window and hard-failed with `[B67.1][asset_class=crypto_spot] missing module_constants in macro_modifier module: b67_1_asset_class_no_op_active.` until the pm2 restart at 17:56 picked up the new code. This is BY-DESIGN behavior per §5 #10 (no silent fallback) + §5 #15 (NO PATCHES) — explicit retry on next timer tick is correct. But creates 2-6 minutes of WARN spam during deploys that add new module_constants rows. **Fix shape (Tier 3 polish, not blocking):** deploy-runbook entry that orders migration BEFORE the build/restart pair (already happens in current step order; the issue is that the OLD running process exercises the NEW constant requirement before the restart finishes). Alternative: pm2 stop dawntrader BEFORE npm run db:migrate, then run migration + build + pm2 start (cold-start variant). Out-of-batch fix candidate. | OPEN — Tier 3, deploy-procedure polish | Surfaced 2026-05-25 by Langston during B79.0n.CONFIDENCE-CHAIN Step 8 second-pass verification. |

| 135-ORIG | (Original 2026-05-22 surface, preserved for reference) Surfaced 2026-05-22 by Kyle. The system-alerts mechanism (`/var/log/dawntrader/system-alerts.jsonl` + the `fire-due` dispatcher cron) is a passive queue: the dispatcher promotes a scheduled alert to `state=active` (sets `fired_at`) when `triggers_at` passes — but it does NOT post to Telegram, message CC, or invoke Langston. The dispatcher log shows only `[fire-due] promoted N alert(s) to active`. The only "notification" path is the §10.5 per-turn pull check — every Claude Code session tails the alerts file and surfaces due entries. **Two coverage gaps:** (a) the §10.5 check only runs while a CC session is actively in progress — if no session is running when an alert fires, it waits unsurfaced until the next session starts; (b) Langston is structurally unable to catch a scheduled alert on his own — he is not a persistent process, he runs only when invoked via `claude -p`, so between invocations there is no Langston to run the §10.5 check. Empirical: d4b2e590 (2026-05-22 UD 24h regression) was caught only because CC was in an active session + Kyle surfaced a screenshot; Langston was not notified and could not have been. **Fix shape:** when the `fire-due` dispatcher promotes an alert to active it should also actively push — (1) post the alert to Telegram topic 21 via `cc-comms-bridge` so Kyle sees it, and (2) optionally invoke Langston via SSH+`claude -p` with the alert body so a Langston session actually runs and performs the §10.5 surfacing. Small, self-contained dispatcher enhancement — candidate for a quick standalone batch. | OPEN — Tier 3, alert-infra enhancement | Surfaced 2026-05-22 by Kyle question — the alert system did not actively notify CC or Langston of d4b2e590; the §10.5 pull-check is the sole path and has the two coverage gaps above. |

### #160 OPEN 2026-05-28 — TFS confidence-formula `momentumFactor` saturates above 1% xStock momentum (Phase 25 calibration input)
B-XSTOCK-CALIB B.1a archive replay (2,658 bars / 260 symbols) surfaced a structural characteristic of the TFS confidence formula on xStock data. The formula `confidence = tfsDesatMin + (tfsDesatMax - tfsDesatMin) × (momentumFactor × dbsStrength × volInverse)` computes `momentumFactor = clamp01(mom / tfsMomentumScale)` with xstock_spot's `tfsMomentumScale = 0.010`. So momentumFactor saturates at 1.0 once raw momentum exceeds 1%. xStock momentum p95 from the replay = 6.0%, so the saturation point is well below the 95th percentile of TFS-eligible bars. Practical effect: for the bulk of TFS-classified xStock bars, the formula's dynamic range collapses to `dbsStrength × volInverse` only — momentum is at the ceiling and not contributing to confidence differentiation. **Not a B.1 bug, not a B.1 fix** — the multiplicative formula structure with these scale values is intentional ("all three inputs must align" per B67.3.5 design). Phase 25 calibration cycle (when paper-trade outcomes are available) is the correct loop to decide whether momentumFactor saturation hurts trade selection — if high-confidence TFS labels predict winning trades only weakly, formula rescaling for xStock becomes justified at that point. Until then, capture as a structural observation. Full analysis in `Claude Comms and Packages/Cross-Session Briefs/B_1A_DISTRIBUTION_ANALYSIS.md` §2.1 + §5 Phase-25-handoff. | OPEN — Phase 25 calibration input | Surfaced 2026-05-28 during B-XSTOCK-CALIB B.1a archive replay.

**Resolution:** B-4.7 chunk A (deployed `b8ab812de`, 2026-06-11) deleted BOTH mixed-class dominant-regime votes (MCE + telemetry-aggregator) and replaced them with `getDominantRegimeForClass(assetClass)` (MIN 5 same-class pairs, null below = CLASS_IDLE); `getMarketIndicators(assetClass)` REQUIRED with per-class regime/friction/DBS and an explicit `voteStatus` LIVE|IDLE_OR_WARMING marker; per-class transition tracking with idle suppression + silent re-seed (independent friction idle flag per Langston diff-A R1); VTS stamps class-true `globalRegime`/`globalFriction`/`globalDirectionalBias` with at-open preservation (close-time re-resolution removed); per-class API payloads + two-badge UI. #### Original #162 entry (historical)
Surfaced by Kyle 2026-05-29 during B.1.5 Step 3 downstream-consumer discussion. Verified in code: `market-context-engine.ts:1624` `MCE.getDominantRegime()` iterates the entire MCE per-symbol cache (which post-B79.0n.MCE contains BOTH `crypto_spot` AND `xstock_spot` entries, keyed `${symbol}:${assetClass}`) and aggregates everything into ONE dominant regime via majority-vote — **no asset-class filter** anywhere in the function. Consumed by `getMarketIndicators()` (market-indicators.ts:268) → Signal Orchestrator (market indicators + ranking context bonus per SIM §5.5/§4.1). **This is architecturally inconsistent with the rest of the per-class boundary the system has built:** Global DBS = per-class (separate `xstockDirectionalBiasStore` + crypto store, B-PHASE-A2); friction / cost-model = per-class (`XSTOCK_SPOT_FRICTION` + crypto friction); MCE per-pair context = per-class (cache key + REQUIRED-assetClass on confidence-chain); regime classifier itself = per-class thresholds (B-XSTOCK-CALIB B.1 validated xstock_spot regime distribution). **Behavioral concern:** crypto markets and equity markets have different regime dynamics, and a mixed-class dominant regime probably reflects neither well. With current rotation (~75 xStock + ~150-200 crypto per cycle), the crypto cohort likely dominates the vote → xStock signals get scored against a crypto-flavored global regime context. **Fix direction:** split `getDominantRegime()` into per-class variants (`getDominantRegimeForClass(assetClass)`) mirroring the per-class accessor pattern from B79.0n.MCE + B79.0n.CONFIDENCE-CHAIN; update `getMarketIndicators()` to call the right one per consumer site. Out of scope for B.1.5 (would touch shared MCE + indicator-consumer plumbing). Candidates: small standalone batch (B-NEW-N), OR Phase 25 calibration item, OR Phase 19 if it surfaces as a calibration blocker during active-paper restoration. **SEQUENCING LOCKED (Kyle directive 2026-05-30):** scheduled as **B-NEW-48**, sequenced AFTER B-NEW-47 (storage). CONDITIONAL on a consumer-impact check at B-NEW-48 scoping: audit whether `getMarketIndicators().dominantRegime` materially influences Signal Orchestrator (active-trading path), VTS Runner (passive learning path), and the ranking context bonus for xStock signals — i.e. does the mixed-class dominant regime change xStock outcomes vs. a per-class one? If material → build B-NEW-48. If marginal → defer further or fold into Phase 25. | OPEN — B-NEW-48 scheduled (after B-NEW-47), conditional on consumer-impact audit | Surfaced 2026-05-29 by Kyle during B.1.5 Step 3; sequencing locked 2026-05-30.

### #165 CLOSED 2026-06-01 (B-NEW-50) — node-cron 4.2.1 `getNextRun()` wrong for day-of-week schedules ≥~2 days out (introspection-only; firing safe)
Surfaced by B-NEW-49 boot smoke test on first deploy. `weekend_shutdown` schedule (cron expression `0 20 * * 5`, timezone `America/New_York`) calls `task.getNextRun()` and receives `2027-01-02T00:00:00.000Z` instead of the expected `2026-06-06T00:00:00.000Z` (next Friday 8 PM ET = Sat June 6 00:00 UTC). Off by ~215 days. `weekend_restart` (`0 20 * * 0` same timezone) returns correct date — so the bug is specific to Friday day-of-week (5) interaction with `America/New_York` timezone, not a general timezone issue.

**★ 2026-06-10 root-cause candidate (B-4.6-B pre-audit, Langston-confirmed):** the 2026-06-09 cron misses (13:00 hourly + 18:00 reflection, same PID, self-cleared) carry the **scan-loop microtask-starvation signature** — warm-cache awaits yield only microtasks, so an unbroken warm sweep starves the timer queue and the cron slot ticks past (`B_4_6B_PRE_AUDIT.md` §1). **B-4.6-B chunk B is the durable-fix candidate**; its post-deploy soak checks cron-miss counts as a secondary acceptance signal.

**★ 2026-06-12 ATTRIBUTION SHARPENED (B-4.6-B chunk B shipped — FIX-2026-06-12-C):** the dominant starvation source was NOT the scan compute but the Batch-44 `persistDiagnostics` 20-30MB sync disk write every 30s sweep (DELETED per Kyle's legacy ruling); the scan loops' contiguous compute was the secondary contributor (fixed by boundary yields). Once-per-sweep stall ELIMINATED (~120 stalls/hour → ~8/hour scattered singles; corrected full-window figures in FIX-2026-06-12-C item 5). Zero actual cron misses in the post-fix window (verifier no-ops only — Langston Step-8 verified). Cron-miss disappearance over the formal 24h gate window (re-issued alert; fires 2026-06-13T15:00Z) is the confirming evidence for this family.

**Important uncertainty:** unknown whether (a) ONLY the `getNextRun()` introspection API is buggy while actual firing happens at the correct time, OR (b) actual firing is ALSO wrong and Fri timer will never fire correctly until node-cron is fixed/replaced. The B-NEW-36 weekend-shutdown miss on Fri 29 May is consistent with hypothesis (b) but not definitive — the May 29 miss could also have been from the BUG-2026-05-31-B silent-failure pattern.

**Investigation plan:**
1. Reproduce in isolation: write a 10-line test script that registers `cron.schedule('0 20 * * 5', cb, { timezone: 'America/New_York' })` and logs `getNextRun()` immediately. Confirm bug reproduces independent of our codebase.
2. Test other day-of-week values + other timezones to characterize: does `0 20 * * 1-4` (Mon-Thu) also return wrong dates? Does `0 20 * * 5` in UTC return correct dates?
3. Check node-cron 4.2.1 issue tracker for similar reports; if reproducer holds, file upstream.
4. Decide: pin known-good version, replace library, OR add timezone-aware getNextRun() shim.

**Why not blocker for B-NEW-49 ship:** the smoke test wrote an alert as designed; this IS exactly what the batch was built to catch. The B-NEW-36 weekend boundary is robust to node-cron failures regardless (poll-reconcile on independent centralClock). Sunday-resume tonight (Mon 1 Jun 00:00 UTC) uses the correctly-armed weekend_restart timer (returned correct date). Friday-shutdown next week (Sat 6 Jun 00:00 UTC) has poll-reconcile as the safety net.

**RESOLUTION (B-NEW-50, commit `6372a2d` + hotfix `63bc69d`, deployed + staging-verified 2026-06-01):**
- **Root cause (proven via isolated repro `scratch/ri165-repro.cjs` + node-cron source read):** `MatcherWalker.matchNext()` (matcher-walker.js:84-89) advances the weekday-reconcile loop by a whole YEAR per iteration instead of a day → ANY day-of-week schedule whose next hit is ≥~2 days out returns the next Jan-1st landing on that weekday (Fri→2027, Tue→2030, Sat→2028…), in BOTH NY and UTC. **Broader than originally framed** (not Friday/NY-specific; the smoke test only flagged Friday because Sunday's next fire was imminent and computed correctly).
- **Uncertainty RESOLVED = hypothesis (a): introspection-only.** Firing uses a SEPARATE path — `TimeMatcher.match(now)` predicate + a 24h heartbeat-delay cap (runner.js:178) — which self-corrects as the real fire-time enters the ~24h window. Proven empirically: `match(Fri 8PM ET)=true`, `getNextMatch(from Wed)=2027` (same TimeMatcher); a LIVE day-of-week-constrained schedule fired at the exact scheduled second (`ri165-confirm.cjs`). **The May 29 Friday non-fire was the 30-hour staging outage (process down), NOT this bug.** Live confirmation: the Sun 2026-06-01 00:00 UTC `weekend_restart` fired correctly via cron (`src=cron`), resuming 244 trades.
- **Fix = SHIM (Langston Step-1 + Step-4 ACK):** new `server/services/cron-next-fire.ts` `computeNextFire()` backed by `cron-parser` (promoted to direct dep @4.9.0); arm-logger + smoke-test classify on it; node-cron's raw `getNextRun()` kept only as labelled `[UNTRUSTED ncv=4.2.1]` diagnostic. node-cron scheduling/firing untouched. Verified on staging: smoke test reports weekend_shutdown next_fire=2026-06-06, weekend_restart=2026-06-08, aggregate OK 7/7; 4 stale TOO_FAR_FUTURE alerts acknowledged.
- **Deploy lesson (→ BUG-2026-06-01-A):** `import { parseExpression } from 'cron-parser'` (named) passes tsc+vitest+CI-build but CRASHES the production esbuild `--format=esm` bundle (CJS named-export not statically detectable). Fixed via default-import. Generalizable verification-gap learning logged in ASSET_CLASS_ONBOARDING_WORKFLOW.

**Sequence:** CLOSED. (Originally slated post-B-NEW-47; pulled forward to FIRST per Kyle 2026-05-31.)

### #166 OPEN 2026-05-31 — TEC stale-cache fence still firing post-B-NEW-40 (3,716 events in 14-day soak)
Surfaced by B-NEW-40 14-day soak verification script (alert `b83b1e4b`, fired Sat 31 May 12:58 UTC, manually run during B-NEW-49 governance close). Script verdict: **FAIL** — 3,716 `TEC_STALE_FAIL_CLOSED` events detected in the 14-day window post-deploy `2026-05-17T12:46:47Z`. Distribution: May 18 (2,368 events), May 19 (964), May 23 (50), May 25 (30), May 26 (304). Script says "DO NOT auto-ack. Manual review required."

**What's happening:** TEC = Trailing Exit Controller. Per-asset-class kill-switch flags (e.g., "is break-even disabled for xstock_spot?") are cached in-memory and refreshed from `module_constants` on a schedule. B-NEW-40 added a 5-minute staleness fence — if the cache is older than 300s, system refuses to honor it and fails-closed (logs `TEC_STALE_FAIL_CLOSED`). The fence is doing its job — refusing to use stale values. The PROBLEM is the underlying staleness is recurring: the cache IS getting stale, repeatedly.

**Likely cause (per soak script verdict + B-NEW-40 design):** database connection going dead-but-ESTABLISHED (TCP keepalive not detecting the dead socket, refresh query hangs until timeout, cache ages past 300s ceiling). Investigation steps per the script: check `/var/log/dawntrader/tec_diag/` for `pg_stat_activity` + `ss` snapshots from the incidents; query `/api/diagnostics/tec-config` for live state of inFlight Map + last-success timestamps; if `ss` shows dead-but-ESTABLISHED sockets → tighten `keepAliveInitialDelayMillis`; if `ss` shows clean sockets but TEC still stale → NEW failure mode B-NEW-40 didn't cover → escalate.

**Active-trading impact:** ZERO today (paper_sim_trades + trades both empty). Fence failing-closed means the system errs on the safe side (refuses to act on potentially-wrong cached value rather than acting incorrectly). When active-paper trading restarts at Phase 19, the TEC stale-cache issue becomes operationally relevant — needs to be fixed before that point.

**Alert state:** ACTIVE, unacknowledged. Stays active until investigated (per script's own instruction).

**Sequencing:** investigate as separate batch — different surface entirely from B-NEW-49 (DB connection layer, not cron). Likely next after B-NEW-49 ships and Sunday-resume verification completes. Could be folded with #165 if both are short — but #166 needs Langston attention given prior context on B-NEW-40 design.

Boot 22:26:36 UTC clean. First post-hotfix verifier run at 22:41:36 UTC will validate no false-positive recurs. Original entry below preserved for context.

### #167 (original OPEN entry — superseded by CLOSED above)
Surfaced at first verifier run after B-NEW-49 deploy (22:12:48 UTC). The verifier alerted on `feed_integrity_cron` with reason=`no_fires_ever_past_boot_grace` (lastFire was null in the query result), but the DB actually had 4 successful `feed_integrity_cron` rows in `scheduled_tasks_audit` at 22:00, 22:05, 22:10, 22:15 UTC. Alert `c6e35132-ca39-4817-be5d-897bc4923e20` ack'd as false-positive.

**Root cause:** `cron-fire-evidence-verifier.ts:queryLastFires` uses `WHERE task_name = ANY(${jobNames}::text[])` in a Drizzle SQL template. Drizzle's parameter escaping for a JS array against a Postgres text[] cast does not produce the expected ANY-match — query silently returns zero rows.

**Fix (small hotfix):** swap the SQL to either (a) drop the WHERE filter entirely and filter in JS (`SELECT task_name, MAX(fired_at) FROM scheduled_tasks_audit GROUP BY task_name` then JS-filter against `cronRegistry.getAll()`), OR (b) use Drizzle's `inArray(scheduledTasksAudit.taskName, jobNames)` operator. Lean is (a) — N=~10 task_names in the table, JS filter trivial, no Drizzle import gymnastics.

**Test coverage gap:** the existing `cron-fire-evidence-verifier.test.ts` mock returns rows correctly because it bypasses the real SQL escape path. Add a real-DB integration test OR fix the unit-test mock to simulate Drizzle's actual array-param behavior.

**Sequencing:** small hotfix (~30 min). Should land BEFORE B-NEW-47 storage so the safety net is trustworthy before more deploys. First post-compaction action.

**Operational impact:** verifier produces false positives every 15 min for healthy schedules. Each false positive writes an alert that the dispatcher pushes to Telegram + invokes Langston via SSH. Langston already responded to one false alert + recommended restart (per his protocol — he didn't know it was a query bug). Until fixed, EVERY 15 min the verifier will re-alert on ALL 7 schedules + spam the alert queue. The MODE-A smoke test is unaffected (uses `task.getNextRun()` not DB query) — the real weekend_shutdown bug it caught is still legitimate.

### #168 OPEN 2026-06-01 (Langston B-NEW-50 Step-8 flag) — CI cannot catch production-bundle boot crashes (ESM/CJS-interop gap)
**Surfaced by B-NEW-50 (BUG-2026-06-01-A):** `import { parseExpression } from 'cron-parser'` (named import of a CommonJS-only package) passed **tsc + vitest + CI Build (esbuild) + Docker Build — all four green** — then HARD-CRASHED the app at staging boot: `SyntaxError: Named export 'parseExpression' not found` from the production `esbuild --format=esm --packages=external` bundle (Node's ESM loader can't statically detect CJS named exports). ~3-min staging outage; hotfixed via default-import (`63bc69d`).

**The gap:** none of the 4 CI gates execute the production bundle's runtime. CI Build only *bundles*; it never boots the artifact. So any future named import from a CJS package will sail through a fully-green pipeline and only surface at deploy. This is a structural pipeline hole, not a cron-parser quirk.

**Recommended fix (Langston, NO-PATCHES):** add a CI step that runs the actual production esbuild bundle in a headless boot smoke — require the process to reach "listening" / emit the `[CRON-ARM-SMOKE] aggregate=` boot line within a timeout — before CI is marked green. Closes the class structurally. Alternatively/additionally: a lint rule flagging named imports from known-CJS packages.

**Process learning (also logged in ASSET_CLASS_ONBOARDING_WORKFLOW):** for any batch adding a NEW external dependency import, validate the production ESM bundle import form (default-import for CJS) or boot the bundle locally before deploy — green tsc+vitest+CI is necessary but NOT sufficient.

**Sequence:** small CI batch; do after B-NEW-47 storage. Active-trading impact zero. Active alert `b83b1e4b` (#166 TEC soak) remains intentionally deferred per Kyle (after Phase 24 xStock calibration).

### #169 OPEN 2026-06-01 (B-NEW-47) — `context-bridge-log-ttl.ts` buffered warm upload latent OOM (deferred)
B-NEW-47 fixed the buffered-upload OOM in the b75 retention sweep + rehydrate (streaming `uploadWarmFile`/`downloadWarmFile`), but `context-bridge-log-ttl.ts` (a SEPARATE sweep for the `context_bridge_log` table) still uses the buffered `uploadWarm`/`downloadWarm` + `fs.readFileSync` (lines :177/:186/:187). **Real mitigation = table-size only:** `context_bridge_log` stays small (3 warm + 1 cold manifest rows, ~16 MB warm). The existing `413→cold` fallback does **NOT** protect against OOM — `Buffer.from(await res.arrayBuffer())` OOMs *before* any upload that would 413. Concrete trigger: a monthly `context_bridge_log` partition whose gzipped export approaches available RAM (~2.7 GB free on the 3.7 GB box) would OOM the buffered read. Not reachable today (bridge-log volume is tiny) AND the sweep isn't even scheduled. **Fix when it matters:** migrate ctx-bridge-ttl to the streaming `uploadWarmFile`/`downloadWarmFile` path (low effort — methods already exist). | OPEN — deferred, table-size-bounded | Surfaced + deferred per Langston Step-2 Q-D.

### #170 OPEN 2026-06-01 (B-NEW-47) — day-grain is the floor: a single day-slice > 5 GB compressed would permanently stall its partition
B-NEW-47 slices oversized monthly partitions per-DAY. There is **no sub-day fallback**: if one calendar day's compressed export ever exceeds the 5 GB Supabase per-object cap (`HARD_CAP_BYTES`), that slice fails its pre-upload size check, never reaches `verified`, the DROP gate never passes, and the sweep fires a `warning` system-alert every night for that partition forever (data stays safe in the un-dropped hot partition). Not reachable at current volume (~300–500 MB/day for the 31 GB May spot-ticker → ~17–30 day-slices). Documented boundary, not a bug. **If ever hit:** add hour-grain slicing below the day, OR raise the per-object ceiling if Supabase project cap is lifted. | OPEN — documented limit, not reachable now | Surfaced + accepted per Langston Step-2 fold-in #3.

### #171 OPEN 2026-06-01 (B-NEW-47) — corrupt month+day manifest coexistence = MANUAL intervention (runbook, not auto-retry)
The sweep's `deriveModeFromLabels` resume guard hard-throws if a partition has BOTH a `YYYY-MM` (whole) AND `YYYY-MM-DD` (sliced) warm manifest row — a should-never-happen mixed state (the mode is decided once and the invariant guard overrides the live threshold on resume). If it ever trips, the sweep fails that partition every night with the same throw — **it needs a human, not a retry.** **Runbook:** inspect `SELECT partition_label, state FROM data_archive_manifest WHERE source_table=<t> AND (partition_label='<YYYY-MM>' OR partition_label LIKE '<YYYY-MM>-%')`; determine which set is genuine (download-verify it), delete the spurious manifest row(s) + their warm object(s), then let the next sweep resume cleanly. | OPEN — should-never-happen, manual-only | Surfaced per Langston Step-2 fold-in #C.

### #173 OPEN 2026-06-01 (F-NOW, Langston A3-2) — consider a RECURRING zero-NULL forward-path guard once Phase-25 consumes the calibration_state dataset
F-NOW's writer is **fail-open**: when `resolveCalibrationState` returns null (DB error / missing parent / undefined `vtsOpenTradeId`), the alternates row is written with `calibration_state = NULL`, which `IS DISTINCT FROM <tag>` **includes** in the aggregation — i.e., a lookup failure leaks a pre-calibration row INTO the Phase-25 dataset rather than conservatively excluding it. For F-NOW the dataset gates no decision, and the one-time Step-8 zero-NULL check (`xstock_spot AND vts AND calibration_state IS NULL AND created_at > deploy_ts` = 0) is the detection net. **When Phase 25 actually gates a calibration decision on this dataset,** consider promoting that check to a RECURRING system-alert guard (e.g., a daily probe) so a silent forward-path regression can't quietly contaminate a real decision. Detection-with-a-net is acceptable now; silent-and-undetected would not be. | OPEN — deferred to Phase 25 (no decision gated yet) | Surfaced 2026-06-01 in Langston F-NOW Step-4 review A3-2.

### #198 OPEN 2026-06-02 (B-NEW-51, Langston Step-4) — cron-fire-evidence verifier `no_fires_ever` edge for a newly-registered cron whose first occurrence is already past
The B-NEW-51 cadence-aware verifier flags a job stale when `lastFire === null` AND its most-recent scheduled occurrence is older than the boot-grace window. For an EXISTING job this is correct (never-fired = real failure). But a NEWLY-registered cron added mid-cycle — e.g. a daily `0 9 * * *` added at 3pm whose 9am occurrence already passed — has a `prevOccurrence` predating its own existence and would flag `no_fires_ever` ONCE before its first real fire. Blast radius tiny: B-NEW-51 dedup means exactly ONE keyed alert and it auto-clears on first successful fire. **Cannot trigger today** — all 7 currently-registered jobs have fire history (lastFire never null). Clean close = add a `registered_at` floor to `cron-registry` + skip staleness when `prevOccurrence < registered_at`. Not built (no current trigger; would touch the registry interface + ~7 registration sites — disproportionate now). Build it when a new infrequent schedule is added. **Note 2026-06-06 (B-NEW-52):** the two infrequent weekly schedules (`weekend_shutdown`/`weekend_restart`) that motivated the verifier's calendar-aware staleness work have now been RETIRED entirely — the xStock weekend lifecycle is driven by the continuous 30s poll-reconcile + boot-reconcile, not by `node-cron`. The verifier auto-deregistered them (dynamic `cronRegistry.getAll()` expected-set), so the remaining 5 schedules are all frequent (sub-daily) and unlikely to hit the `no_fires_ever` edge. This closes the practical weekend-job angle of the #164/#165/#198 cron saga; the generic `registered_at`-floor edge stays OPEN for any future infrequent schedule. | OPEN — known edge, no current trigger (weekend jobs retired B-NEW-52) | Surfaced 2026-06-02 in Langston B-NEW-51 Step-4 review.

### #201 OPEN 2026-06-04 (B.4 foundation, Langston-surfaced) — RE-CHARACTERIZED 2026-06-05: `range_trade` is starved at the decision substrate (selection effect, NOT a forming-bar artifact) (EV leakage)
**⚠️ Original premise FALSIFIED + corrected (CC + Langston independent verification 2026-06-05, B.5 reconciliation).** The original text claimed "the live forming bar inflates intrabar range, so the live regime classifier almost never lands in RANGE_BOUND_STABLE (≈0%), starving `mean_reversion`/`range_trade`/`vwap_bounce`/`support_bounce`." Two-substrate measurement at 15-min bars (June 1–5, xstock_spot) shows this is wrong on both the mechanism and the breadth:
- **`pair_scan_archive`** (per-pair, every scan cycle — same forming-bar exposure as live): RANGE_BOUND = **6.28%** (healthy, ≈ predicted 6.6%). The regime CLASSIFICATION is fine.
- **`signal_eval_archive`** (decision substrate — only moments a signal is evaluated): RANGE_BOUND = **0.0391%**. This is where the old "≈0%" came from.
- **Breadth is NARROW, not the whole "range family":** only **`range_trade`** is starved — 848 evals all month, **100% in RANGE_BOUND**, hard-gated to a regime that is 0.04% of decision moments. `mean_reversion` is HIGH_VOLATILITY_UNSTABLE-native (~24k evals) and `vwap_bounce` is IMPULSE_EXPANSION-native (~50k evals) — NOT starved, ample sample. `support_bounce` is disabled.
- **Mechanism is selection/coverage, NOT forming-bar reclassification:** pair_scan has identical forming-bar exposure yet stays healthy at 6.28%, so the collapse to 0.04% is the scanner + one-best-per-cycle orchestration surfacing movers (range_trade is the only thing gated to quiet ranges → it starves). → the original "settle-before-classify / forming-bar-aware" fix direction is **likely WRONG**; the leak is downstream gating/selection.
- **Decisive test BEFORE designing the fix (Phase 19):** join `signal_eval_archive ⋈ pair_scan_archive` on (symbol, 15-min bucket) and compare `regime_label` — broad agreement ⇒ pure selection (fix in orchestration/coverage); systematic disagreement ⇒ a forming-bar reclass component exists. Don't commit a fix until this join is run.
- **Constrains B.5 W2.2:** `range_trade` gets its own evidence sub-step and likely lands INCONCLUSIVE under the min-sample gate (848 samples), not a fragile fit. | OPEN — `range_trade` selection-starvation, EV-leakage, fix-direction needs the join-test (Phase 19) | Surfaced 2026-06-04 (B.4 regime-recalib, Langston); re-characterized 2026-06-05 (B.5 two-substrate reconciliation, CC + Langston).

### #202 OPEN 2026-06-04 (B.4 foundation) — deploy-hygiene: on-staging study scripts leave git-tree artifacts that block the next `git pull`; runtime-generated files are git-tracked
Running analysis / study / recompute scripts directly on the staging checkout (as the B.4 replay + DBS-recompute work required) leaves the staging git working tree dirty — modified tracked files plus untracked output files — which blocks the next deploy's `git pull` until manually cleared (`git checkout -- <paths>` + `rm <untracked>`). Separately, several directories that are RUNTIME-generated (`logs/`, `audit/`, `bridge/`, `diagnostics/`) are git-tracked and accrue modifications during normal operation, compounding the dirty-tree problem on every deploy. **Fix direction:** (a) a documented convention to run study scripts from a scratch dir / clean them before deploy, and/or (b) add the runtime-generated directories to `.gitignore` (after confirming nothing load-bearing is tracked there). Operational hygiene, not a correctness bug — but it makes deploys fragile. **RECURRED 2026-06-06 (B-NEW-52 deploy):** the staging tree was again dirty (runtime-tracked `audit`/`bridge`/`diagnostics`/`logs` + a leftover `scripts/b5-w2a-geometry-sweep.ts` scp'd by a prior study) and aborted the first `git pull`; cleared with `git checkout -- audit bridge diagnostics logs` + `rm` then re-pulled clean. **2nd occurrence in 2 deploys — elevate to fix:** the `.gitignore` route (b) for the four runtime dirs is now the clear priority (a one-time `git rm --cached` + `.gitignore` add); ad-hoc clearing on every deploy is the failure mode this issue predicted. | OPEN — deploy-hygiene cleanup, ELEVATED after 2nd occurrence | Surfaced 2026-06-04 during B.4 foundation activation deploy; recurred 2026-06-06 (B-NEW-52).

### #204 OPEN 2026-06-05 (HCE study, Langston-surfaced) — xStock corrupt-stop-price (units/scale) incidence ~45x higher than crypto
The Hidden-Contextual-Edge study's data-integrity pass found 25 VTS trades (of 22,810) with a corrupt entry-stop price — `signal.stopLoss` more than 50% off the entry price (e.g. STX entry $0.2518 with stop $818.93, an unreachable units-scale-wrong stop). The split is **21 xStock + 4 crypto** — ~0.9% xStock incidence vs ~0.02% crypto, **~45x higher in xStock**. Excluding the 25 from the study is correct for analysis, but a rate asymmetry that large points at an xStock-specific price-scale / precision-handling issue (fractional-share magnitudes, a canonicalization step, or a write-time scale bug) that mis-scales the stop on a small fraction of xStock fills. **Why it matters beyond analysis:** if entry/stop units mis-scale on a LIVE xStock fill it bites EXECUTION (a stop placed ~3000x away never protects), not just back-analysis — so it must be resolved before the multi-asset active path leans harder on xStock. Note: the corrupt field is `signal.stopLoss`; `originalStopPrice` was found reliable (only 3 corrupt of 22,810). **Next:** reproduce the 21 xStock cases (mostly `strong_bull_trend`), determine whether it is a write-time scale bug vs a read/parse issue, fix before Phase 19 active-paper. | OPEN — xStock data-integrity, Phase-A / Phase-19 (pre-active-trading) | Surfaced 2026-06-05 in the HCE study geometry-rider pass; Langston flagged the 45x asymmetry as a likely deeper issue under a small anomaly.

### #205 OPEN 2026-06-05 (HCE study, Langston-confirmed) — rejected-arm causal test (net-EV over-rejection) DEFERRED: blocked on intraday-data coverage
The HCE study's causal layer — does the net-EV gate (`net_ev_rejected`, 5,068 May–Jun signals) reject signals that would have been net-positive? — is the highest-value follow-on, because the admitted arm is robustly null (no within-survivor context edge), so any remaining edge lives at the **admission boundary**. The managed-exit reconstruction tool (`scripts/hce/hce_ohlc_sim.py`) is BUILT and VALIDATED (Gate (c): 96.8% win/loss sign-match, 0.000% median net-error *when bars exist*), but its pre-registered GO/NO-GO bar FAILED on the no-hit criterion (19.1% pooled > 15% cap) — **entirely from crypto 1-min OHLC sparsity** (xStock no-hit 1% = GO-quality; crypto 31%; the failure is missing bars, not a broken sim). Rejected signals are disproportionately thin-liquidity alts (~53% OHLC coverage, sparse bars) — worse than admitted. So no trustworthy causal number ships, and an xStock-only exploratory delta is explicitly NOT shipped (n=121 over a 5-week-old asset class = anchor risk + answers the wrong question; Langston). **Two data prerequisites to unblock:** (a) a **denser intraday source** — trade-tick reconstruction or order-book history — for the thin crypto symbols, so the first-hit sim stops whiffing on gaps; (b) a **watch on xStock `net_ev_rejected` accrual** (121 today; xStock onboarded 2026-04-30) so the xStock-only causal arm can run faithfully once it reaches power — as its OWN pre-registered mini-study, not a footnote on this one. **RESCHEDULED to Phase 25 (roadmap 25-15, Kyle 2026-06-07)** — labelled with the other data-gap-blocked calibration studies so the record shows it was attempted. | OPEN — deferred, data-blocked; rescheduled Phase 25 (25-15); highest-value analysis follow-on | Surfaced 2026-06-05 in HCE study P2 NO-GO; Langston confirmed the deferral + both prerequisites; rescheduled to Phase 25 per Kyle 2026-06-07.

### #206 OPEN 2026-06-06 (B.5 W2.0b, Langston-directed) — decision-provenance NOT persisted on `signal_eval_archive` → backward replay can't reproduce live decisions; spec a GENERAL forward capture (the 3rd study to hit this wall)
B.5 W2.0b built a detect-replay harness (`scripts/b5-w20b-entry-replay.ts`) to sweep xStock entry-trigger thresholds, behind a hard parity gate (replay must reproduce the live fire/no-fire decision ≥99% before any swept number is trusted). **The harness runs correctly but cannot clear the gate on historical data** — Tier-1 fired/no-fire parity for vwap_pullback maxed at **80%** (1m-rebuild 62% → live-snapshot settled bars 80%; ticker-`last` currentPrice ruled out; constants confound closed — `price_position` gate constants stable since 2026-05-05). The hard diagnosis: the live engine's **exact decision-time inputs were never persisted** — `signal_eval_archive.features` stores only scoring metadata, not the `ohlcData` array or resolved constants. The +18pp from feeding the real 15m snapshot bars proved the settled-bar VWAP was the major reproducible gap; the residual ~20% is the **forming (in-progress) bar**, which the live engine built from a live tick overlay that was never persisted and is irreducible from backward data (chasing it = patch trap §8 #11). **This is the THIRD study to hit this identical wall** — W2.0a Mode-A (geometry anchors not persisted → Mode-B pivot), the RI-a stop-anchor-persistence gap (Phase-19), and now W2.0b. One missing layer, not three coincidences. **Forward fix (Langston-directed, NOT a per-strategy patch):** a one-time **general decision-provenance capture on `signal_eval_archive`** — every archived decision records the exact `ohlcData` array evaluated (settled bars AND the forming bar) + the resolved `module_constants`, as of decision time. Done once at the archive layer, every future parity/calibration study (every strategy, every asset class) becomes exact-replayable — the NO-PATCHES move (solve the class). **Open design question (settle at spec, do not balloon scope):** storage — 240 bars × OHLCV/decision isn't free; hash-and-reference against a canonical bar store, or persist forming-bar + bar-set-ids. **Consequence:** xStock entry-trigger calibration (W2.0b/W2.2/W3-ORB entry dimension) is **DATA-BLOCKED** until this instrument lands + accrues ~weeks of forward data. Geometry (W2.0a) was already keep-baseline, so xStock per-strategy calibration is largely data-blocked — an honest result. **Sequencing:** the CAPTURE is a Phase-19 instrumentation batch (B-NEW-53, roadmap 19-20); the BLOCKED STUDIES it unblocks are rescheduled to **Phase 25** and labelled as Phase-24 xStock-calibration blocks so the record shows they were attempted (roadmap **25-12** entry-trigger sweep / **25-13** faithful geometry reconstruction + RI-a stop-anchor replay / **25-14** per-strategy re-fit + ORB entry-edge + vwap_bounce forward test). **Langston final-read reinforcements (2026-06-06):** (1) **#206 and RI-a are ONE layer, not a bundle** — the spec must state up front that a single decision-provenance capture satisfies both #206 (detect inputs) and RI-a (stop anchors); shipping two persistence mechanisms for one root cause is itself a §8 #11 violation. Settle the storage decision (hash-and-reference canonical bar store vs. persist forming-bar + bar-set-ids) AT SPEC, before any code (§8 #11 document-before-implement). (2) **Defined exit for the data-block (don't let it drift):** once the instrument lands and starts writing provenance rows, set a §10.5 scheduled system-alert keyed on a CONCRETE accrual condition (row-count threshold or a nameable date) that re-surfaces "W2.0b is now backward-replayable — resume the entry-trigger sweep," so the block self-clears instead of relying on memory. Full writeup: `Claude Comms and Packages/Scope Files/B_5_W20b_CONCLUSION.md`. **★ CAPTURE BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-06-07 (B-NEW-53, commit `b1dbb2c43`, CI green, Langston Step-4+Step-8 PASS).** Single unified layer satisfies #206 AND RI-a (`signal_eval_provenance` stores forming bar BY VALUE + settled-bar-set reference + resolved constants hash + resolved stop/target levels). xStock-only at launch (crypto off). The two §10.5 defined-exit alerts are registered: runtime-proof (2026-06-08T01:30Z, tonight) + post-accrual proof-of-capture parity re-run (2026-07-05T12Z) that resurfaces "resume the Phase-25 entry-trigger sweep (25-12)". The CAPTURE half is now CLOSED-pending-tonight's-runtime-proof; the STUDY half stays DATA-BLOCKED until the forward data accrues (Phase-25). | PARTIALLY RESOLVED — capture instrument BUILT/DEPLOYED 2026-06-07; study data-blocked until accrual (Phase-25, alert-gated). | Surfaced 2026-06-06 in B.5 W2.0b parity gate; Langston-directed general scope; capture shipped B-NEW-53 2026-06-07.

### #207 RESOLVED (code) 2026-06-08 (B-NEW-53.1, deployed) — was OPEN 2026-06-07 (B-NEW-53 crypto-enable, Langston-flagged) — `Phase10TradeRecord` admitted-`features` block archived UNDEFINED economics on crypto admitted rows (latent B70.2 bug)
Surfaced while wiring B-NEW-53 crypto provenance. `Phase10TradeRecord` (`vts-runner.ts:469`) declares `entry` (NOT `entryPrice`) and does **not** declare `stopLoss`/`takeProfit`/`quantity`/`expectedEdge`/`atrAtOpen`/`pairIdHash`/`regimeConfidenceRaw`/`macroModifierValue`/`phase`/`phaseAgeSeconds`/`strategyPhaseWeight`; the literal at `L1598` sets `entry: entryPrice` and stops there. So the B70.2 admitted-`features` JSONB block (`vts-runner.ts` ~L1953-1986) reads ~12 fields off a record that never carries them → `undefined` → `JSON.stringify` drops the keys → **every crypto admitted row in `signal_eval_archive.features` has hollow economics (null entry/stop/target/quantity/expectedEdge/atr/phase) since B70.2 (2026-05-05)**. The TS2339 baseline silently absorbed it. NOT cosmetic: B-NEW-53 turns crypto capture ON precisely to harvest CLEAN admitted decision data for Phase-25, so a growing window of hollow admitted-`features` is exactly the contamination we are avoiding. **B-NEW-53 provenance is UNAFFECTED** — `signal_eval_provenance` sources the real detect-output `stopLoss`/`takeProfit` locals (L1121-1123), not `tradeRecord`, so Phase-25 replay has the correct levels. xStock `eval-cycle.ts` admitted-`features` are NOT affected (they capture fewer fields). **Fix (Langston-directed, fix-now-but-decouple): B-NEW-53.1 immediate same-day fast-follow, ROOT-CAUSE not patch (§8 #11)** — source each of the ~12 fields from its genuine local at the hook (entry/stop/target trivial = the L1121-1123 locals; the other ~8 need locating at their real source locals), OR extend `Phase10TradeRecord` + its literal to actually carry them; do not paper over with the typed record. **★ RESOLVED (code) 2026-06-08 (B-NEW-53.1, commit `53a208880`, CI all-4-green, deployed to staging):** the admitted-`features` block now reads the 13 fields from the in-scope `persistedTrade` open-trade SSOT (`persistedTrade?.<field> ?? null`) — the record that genuinely carries them — instead of the lean `tradeRecord`; `expectedEdge?: number` added to the `OpenVirtualTrade` interface (already written to the literal L1486 + DB). tsc TS2339 in vts-runner dropped **25→8** (the broken reads were real type errors), vitest no new failures. Langston Step-4 APPROVE-TO-PUSH (choice c; verified handle-in-scope + `?? null` preserves the JSONB key vs `undefined` dropping it). Telemetry-only, NO migration (features JSONB `schema_version`). **Known-NULL window 2026-05-05→2026-06-08 documented in `CHANGES_AND_FIXES.md` — Phase-25 calibration queries MUST exclude/handle it.** **★ LIVE-CONFIRMED 2026-06-08:** the first post-deploy crypto admitted row (ESPORTS/USD `strong_bull_trend`, 02:38Z) populated **all 13 fields at 100%**, sane (entry 0.0826, stop 0.0609, target 0.1260 — stop<entry<target; phase LATE; cohort 0) — vs the deterministic 0/145 blank before; a broader re-confirm system-alert (2026-06-08T14:00Z) re-checks at scale. xStock parallel gap → #208. | RESOLVED (code) 2026-06-08 (B-NEW-53.1); live-data confirm alert-gated | Surfaced 2026-06-07 in B-NEW-53 crypto-enable Step-4; root-cause fixed 2026-06-08.

### #214 OPEN 2026-06-10 (item 4 step-6 throughput study, CC find + Langston code-confirm) — `health_engine` broadcast ENGINE block reads the legacy `global.tradingEngines` registry → lying `isRunning:false` for an actively-trading paper engine, and ZERO paper liveness signal

**#222 ✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-12 (B-5.1, deployed 5737b1ddb at 2026-06-12T01:01:56Z).** Root cause: `market-context-engine.ts:1395` un-class-gated `directionalBiasStore.updatePair` (B63-era write; xstocks began flowing through computeContext at B79.0m.b; the B79.0n.MCE class-keyed cache work missed this write). Fix: `crypto_spot` allowlist on the write. Verified: store refilled pure post-restart — permanent audit probe `probe_dbs_class_purity` PASS (registry-based resolution, n=180, zero non-crypto); class-generic regression test locks it. Baseline-list note: the "24 equity symbols" heuristic overcounted by at least GRASS/USD (genuine Kraken crypto token; registry adjudicates). The weight-cap question (`GLOBAL_DBS_MAX_PAIR_WEIGHT_PCT = 1.0`) was deliberately NOT bundled — separate design question, Phase-19 prep. Original finding: The crypto DirectionalBiasStore holds 24 equity symbols (SPY 9.1%, MU 8.4%, QQQ 7.3%, NVDA 4.6%, ...) carrying **52.6% of total aggregation weight**, with CONSOLIDATED EQUITY-TAPE volumes (SPY at 6.38e10 = NYSE tape, not Kraken tokenized volume) — the "crypto" global DBS is majority-driven by the US equity tape. Measured impact 2026-06-12: weighted median 0.2662 with equities vs 0.2272 without; co-trending markets HIDE the skew (the loose partition-parity check structurally cannot catch it on co-trending days). Compounding: `GLOBAL_DBS_MAX_PAIR_WEIGHT_PCT = 1.0` (per-pair weight cap disabled). Root cause is on the DBS ingest path (writer hygiene — which scanner feeds equity symbols into the crypto store instance, same family as #223). Consumers of crypto global DBS all read the skewed value (AMR weather dbs input, market-indicators display, MCE bias modifier). **Root-cause follow-up required: trace the writer, decide partition (B-PHASE-A2 gave xstock GICS partitioning; crypto store needs the inverse guard), and consider enabling the weight cap.** Home: immediate next governance window / Phase-19 prep (AMR active flip reads this input).

**#223 ✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-12 (B-5.1, deployed 5737b1ddb at 2026-06-12T01:01:56Z).** Writer-side guard shipped at the chokepoint `setCostMetrics` (covers both writers — market-scanner + fx5-scanner): negative `data.spread` dropped at the FIELD level — existing entry keeps prior good spread while sibling fields update; no existing entry → nothing fabricated (`setCostMetrics` returns null; a cache miss is the honest state — stamping DEFAULT_SPREAD would inflate the friction sampler's n with invented data); zero spread (locked book) stays accepted; rejection log throttled once-per-symbol-per-5min. 4-test unit matrix green; all 7 cost-cache reader call sites proven miss-safe at call-site level (pre-audit ADDENDUM table + Langston's independent grep). No live crossed quote in the first post-deploy window (0 rejection lines — episodic event, absence proves nothing; guard armed). Original finding: Root cause: `market-scanner.ts:724` computes `bidAskSpread = ((ask − bid)/bid) × 100` straight from the Kraken ticker; when the book is momentarily CROSSED or one side stale (ask < bid), the spread is NEGATIVE and `setCostMetrics` (cost-cache.ts:85) clamps only the UPPER bound (`Math.min(spread, MAX_COST_BOUND)`) — no lower clamp, so the sentinel persists for up to the 5-min TTL (observed: avgSpread −0.11% across 673 entries pre-B-5). The fx5-scanner path passes spreads through with the same exposure. READ-side guard shipped in B-5 (friction sampling skips `spread < 0`, commit 361544bca); the WRITER-side guard (skip-or-floor crossed quotes — a crossed book is not a measurement) is the structural fix. Small, hot-path — needs its own reviewed change, not a rider. Home: next maintenance batch / Phase-16.

**#225 OPEN 2026-06-12 (B-4.6-B chunk-B close — the pre-declared residual; CARRIES the obj-3 max-clause FAIL) — scattered single-event loop blocks after the scan-stall fix.** With the once-per-sweep 200-700ms block eliminated (FIX-2026-06-12-C), the quantified residual baseline (Langston Step-8 full-window read, CC-reverified, 11.5h / 691 intervals): **96 `[4.6B][STALL]` (≥150ms) events ≈ 8/hour, p50 gap 191ms, max 554ms; 29/691 ELD intervals with max ≥250ms (worst 507.77)** — scattered, no cadence. **Per-event attribution DONE (Kyle-prompted, 2026-06-12 ~20:00Z — all 29 post-final-deploy events bracketed):** (a) **15/29 land immediately after `[FX5Scanner][A3.R7][COMPLETE]`** — the fx5 post-cycle tail still blocks occasionally with persistDiagnostics gone (remaining tail candidates: Stage3Cache update / dataAggregator capture serialization / batch handoff); (b) **7/29 bracketed by the B74 ticker/batch DB writers** — bulk-insert parameter serialization is sync CPU (observed: "xstock_spot flushed 1261 rows"); (c) 7/29 scattered one-offs (WS pong handler, xstock SCAN_CYCLE tail, knowledge-cache sync, heartbeat). **Key property: every named member is BOUNDED per cycle by the fixed pair universe — nothing accumulates over time (the opposite of the deleted Batch-44 grower).** Post-final-deploy rate ≈5/h (29 in ~6h), below the 8/h earlier baseline. Fix direction when worked: chunk/async the bulk-insert serialization + the fx5 tail — the same yield/async family, well-understood. Also in-family: the 72ms single-pair prefetch outlier (soak finding 1). **Gate linkage — RESOLVED BY AMENDMENT (Kyle accepted 2026-06-12):** the obj-3 max<250ms clause was replaced by the **PINNED RESIDUAL CEILING** = this entry's 2026-06-12 baseline (≈8 events/h, p50 191ms, max 554ms, 29/691 intervals ≥250ms). The `[4.6B][STALL]` watchdog + periodic reads TRIP if rate or magnitude exceeds the ceiling — exceeding it reopens this as an active problem, not a tracked leftover. Amendment justification (recorded against precedent-creep, per Langston): the original clause assumed one root cause produced the whole stall profile; that assumption was factually wrong — this family is separate, bounded per cycle, and named. Home: Phase-19 prep (paper debugging wants a quiet loop) / Phase 16.

**#224 ✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-12 (B-5.1, deployed 5737b1ddb at 2026-06-12T01:01:56Z).** Friction WARMING/NO_SOURCE now classifies IDLE (staleness carries `friction_warming`/`friction_no_source`); LOW_VOLUME_THIN and MARKET_CLOSED stay LIVE as-is (measured absence ≠ warm-up). PLUS the Note-3 4th gap found in pre-audit: the amr-gates null-mode branch under `enforce` was allowed/skipped (an ungated ACTIVE-restart window the IDLE-extension would have WIDENED) → now fail-closed `no_posture` block, entry-side only (posture is in-memory-only — no persisted-posture hazard; exits never gated); dry_run/shadow unchanged. Live proof from the deploy restart itself: first ledger cycle both classes IDLE (xstock `friction_warming` — the exact transient that used to stamp false CALM); first LIVE reads CHOPPY→DEFENSIVE (crypto) and STORMY→SURVIVAL (xstock), both ≤ NORMAL per the post-IDLE cap. The session-boundary classification-flapping observation (dwell ladder damping correctly) is NOT closed here — carried by the shadow-week review alert. Original finding: On every restart the xstock weather reads CALM for ~90 seconds (friction store warming → friction input absent → score over remaining inputs) and then flips STORMY once measured spreads arrive (observed at the 20:19, 21:20, 21:40, 22:16 boots on 2026-06-11). Under SHADOW this is a faithful honest-inputs record; under ACTIVE it would open a brief full-size NORMAL-posture window during genuinely hostile (overnight) conditions on every restart. Design question for the Phase-19 flip: hold IDLE (no posture decision) during sentinel warm-up instead of defaulting to a thin-input CALM — interacts with the B5 post-IDLE rule (min(firstRead, NORMAL)) and the input-completeness cap. ALSO from the same ledger evidence: session-boundary classification flapping (CALM 0.699 ↔ STORMY 0.200 within minutes, 19:40–20:20Z) with the dwell ladder correctly damping MODE one rung at a time — the shadow-week review quantifies flip rates and decides whether classification-level hysteresis is needed. Home: Phase 19 (AMR activation design).

**#226 OPEN 2026-06-13 (P19-B1 Step-10 follow-up — Langston Step-2 condition 2 + Step-4 notes A/B) — unit/integration test-tier separation (hermeticity enforcement).** The P19-B1 pattern-filter diagnosis exposed the systemic gap: CI's Postgres service container green-lights unit tests that silently grew real DB dependencies (B-NEW-34's unmocked `getConstant` read survived 28 days invisible because CI satisfied it with a real DB). C-fix repaired the instance; the MASKING MECHANISM survives — the next unit-test hermeticity violation will be green-lit the same way. **Structural completion:** separate vitest projects/tiers — the unit tier runs with NO database reachable so an unmocked read fails fast in CI too; integration/system tiers keep the container. **Acceptance case #1 (Langston Step-4 note B): `b79-0m-b2-pattern-filter.test.ts` must pass on the no-DB unit tier** — the definitive proof the cache seeder (not the DB) satisfies its reads. **Fold-in (Langston Step-4 note A):** TEC `requireKey` checks `=== undefined`; a physically-present row with JSONB `null` flows null into the snapshot silently (pre-existing `pick` semantics, not a regression). No TEC key is legitimately null → flip to `== null` at the next TEC touch or in this batch. **HOME: Phase 20.3.1 (Test Infrastructure) — DECIDED 2026-06-13 by CC + Langston (Kyle-delegated).** Reasoning: it re-plumbs how every test runs (vitest projects/tiers); landing it mid-Phase-19 swaps the foundation while debugging — and the parity bench always having a DB now removes the most likely interim false-pass vector. Optional interim mitigation (do ONLY if ≤~20min, else log as debt): a cheap grep-level CI guard failing any new unit test that imports the DB client without the mock. The `requireKey` `== null` hardening (note A) rides the next TEC touch if sooner. Written into `POST_AUDIT_ROADMAP.md` §20.3.1.

Home: ad-hoc inquiry; revisit only if Kraken's answer opens a hosted-fill path. See P19_B2_COMPLETION_REPORT.md + KNOWN_NONEXISTENT_NAMES (symbol-canonicalizer.ts) Kraken-spot-paper entry. | OPEN (inquiry) |

B3a's widen (`CRYPTO_SPOT_BASE_MAX_LEN=15` SSOT) + the centralized counter in `safeResolveAssetClass` already cover the structural risk for ALL callers; B4 ADDS (a) per-site safe-conversion with active-path skip semantics, (b) registration of the `setClassifyFallthroughHook` escalation hook (system-alert when active trading is ON — the active-vs-passive cut that `shared/` cannot make itself, by design). **Home: P19-B4** (active-path wiring). | OPEN

**#229 OPEN 2026-06-13 (P19-B3a §A6 blast-radius finding — Langston-agreed home) — FOUR separate symbol-form modules with non-identical acceptance (consolidation).** The audit confirmed Kyle's "matched-up-across-all-parts" concern is structural: (1) `shared/asset-classes.ts` (classifier regexes), (2) `server/services/utils/symbol-canonicalizer.ts` (legacy `toCanonical`, hardcoded string-substitution — NO base-length regex, so it does NOT re-introduce the #139 gap; Langston Step-4 scope-check confirmed), (3) `server/markets/kraken-symbol-resolver.ts` (`normalizeInternal` — LOCKED do-not-modify), (4) `server/utils/symbol-normalize.ts` (B79 `normalize`). **#230 OPEN 2026-06-13 (P19-B3a Step-4 — Langston caveat (3), agreed) — fallback-classified VTS samples need a distinguishable tag in the learning store.** B3a switched the 9 vts-runner throwing `resolveAssetClass` sites to `safeResolveAssetClass(...) ?? 'crypto_spot'` — on a genuine fall-through (post-widen, volume ≈ 0) the pair still produces a simulated trade LABELED `crypto_spot` into the exact learning store VTS feeds. It is counted + hooked so observable, but for a passive learner a mislabeled sample silently mixing into clean `crypto_spot` training data is arguably worse than omitting it. **Fix: make fallback-classified samples distinguishable/excludable (a tag, or the escalation hook flags the trade) so they never silently mix into clean training data** — lands with the `setClassifyFallthroughHook` registration. **Home: P19-B4** (with the escalation hook). Alternative considered (Langston): hard-skip-the-symbol (drop the one sample) — cleaner but deferred since post-widen volume ≈ 0. | OPEN

Home: P19-B4** (active-path wiring) / ablation-framework activation. | OPEN

A gate that always passed can now REJECT.
