**#234 OPEN 2026-06-14 (P19-B3b — #137 triage homed buckets, baseline-suppressed) — the ~390 non-active-path tsc errors sorted but NOT fixed in B3b, each with a named home.** Per the Langston-approved P19-B3b triage (`Claude Comms and Packages/Scope Files/P19_B3b_TRIAGE.md`): the 474 baseline tsc errors split into the active-path FIX set (closed in B3b) vs HOMED buckets that stay suppressed in `.tsc-baseline.json`: **routes.ts + routes/vts.ts (200)** → new routes/API type-alignment batch; **storage.ts non-paper-sim 16** → storage/schema + userId-decouple batch; **advisory/cognitive meta — unified-core/autonomy-scheduler+controller/actuation-policy/ethics/reasoning/knowledge-retrieval (67)** → cognitive-subsystem audit (several rule-18 legacy, e.g. unified-core → dropped `ethical_audit_log`); **UI/client (37)** → Phase-20 UI cleanup; **VTS/learning passive incl. the 18 vts-runner telemetry errors (29+18)** → VTS/learning cleanup; **infra/validation/jobs (31)** → infra/test-harness cleanup; **trading-engine + commitTradeAndUpdatePortfolio (4)** → Phase-21 live; **command-router + experience-memory (3, no live callers)** → DELETED_COMPONENTS rule-18 review. None block the active-paper runtime; all are baseline-suppressed (CI green). | OPEN

**#235 OPEN 2026-06-15 (Kyle directive — Phase-19 CRYPTO-pipeline validation) — the crypto active-paper pipeline must be EXPLICITLY validated end-to-end, NOT assumed working.** Last active-paper run = Phase 8 (Nov 2025); ~7 months of accretion since (asset-class awareness, per-class everything, VTS/paper/live separation, new scoring/regime/TEC, multi-class). Crypto is currently covered IMPLICITLY: P19-B3 de-mined the SHARED active path (crypto-inclusive — landmine #2 would have SILENTLY DROPPED EVERY crypto signal at active-paper turn-on; FIXED + observable counter), P19-B4 C2 reuses the shared crypto pipeline (`buildSizedSignalForStrategy`/RTB — exercises it), the B7b switch-on is STAGED **crypto-FIRST** (`crypto_spot first → confirm open→close clean → xstock_spot → rest`), and B9 is the run + first-48h capture audit. MISSING = a DEDICATED, EXPLICIT crypto active-path end-to-end validation: **(a)** a code-level trace/audit of the crypto active chain (FX5 scanner → activeFilterPool → signal-orchestrator `evaluateMarket`→`evaluateSymbol`→`buildSizedSignalForStrategy` → SQE → RTB → TCL/TEC → paper-execution-engine → open→trailing→close) for accreted breakage since Phase 8; **(b)** a controlled crypto-only active dry-run proving the full chain BEFORE the full multi-class flip. **HOME CONFIRMED (CC + Langston consensus 2026-06-15): new dedicated batch P19-B6.5 — crypto resurrection.** Three deliverables (Langston "or the gate is theater"): **(1) ACCRETION-DELTA audit** — diff the shared active path vs the Phase-8 baseline (asset-class awareness + multi-asset pair processing = prime suspects), NOT a generic current-state read; **(2) crypto-only active dry-run observing a FULL closed-trade lifecycle** — scanner→regime→one signal→SQE→RTB→TEC→execution→position OPEN→exit condition→CLOSE→cooldown→telemetry write (NOT just signal emission; force exits or run long enough to watch ≥1 trade close end-to-end); **(3) confirm the fill-path PARITY seam** — per rule-20 this is the paper-execution-engine HIGH-FIDELITY INTERNAL fill (validate=true vetting), NOT a "Kraken paper order system" (Kraken has none for spot — Langston's phrasing corrected). **B7b is HARD-GATED on "B6.5 green"** (explicit checklist written into B7b's entry condition; B7b cannot open until B6.5 closes clean). If B6.5 finds breakage, fixes land in B6.5 or spawn B6.5.x. Langston rationale: SEPARATE JOBS — B6.5 = proof/repair, B7b = the flip — so repair can't balloon under B7b and pressure an early "good enough" flip (§11). Surfaced by Kyle 2026-06-15. **TO FORMALIZE in PHASE_19_PLAN §1 board + §5 decision-log + §6 pre-flight (B7b gate).** | OPEN

**#236 OPEN 2026-06-14 (P19-B4a C3 — Langston review condition 2) — the clock-only xStock liquid-fill-window gate has a US-holiday / half-day HOLE that leaks through the load-bearing safety member.** C3 ships a freshness gate + a clock-based liquid-fill-window gate (RTH 09:30–16:00 ET, DB-resolved) + a silent-stall watchdog. The window gate is purely clock-based and does NOT encode US-equity holidays or early-close half-days (`market-hours.ts` has no holiday calendar — only the 24/5 weekend boundary). FAILURE WALK (Langston): on July 4th at 11:00 ET the clock says in-window → window gate PASSES; the xStock token feed is 24/5 so `captured_at` is fresh → freshness gate PASSES; but ARCA is closed so `last` has not moved in hours → **both gates pass and an active fill lands on a closed-book, stale reference price.** This is the SAME fresh-`captured_at`-but-stale-`last` failure the session gate exists to stop (the C3 Q5 load-bearing case), leaking through a different door. Half-days (early close 13:00 ET) have the identical hole 13:00–16:00. Not shipped-broken in B4a (the whole active dispatch is DORMANT until B7b), but it is a leak in a load-bearing safety member and must be closed before any fill is enabled. **DURABLE FIX (Langston lean, CC agree): a PRICE-DISCOVERY-LIVENESS gate** — require the symbol's `last` price to have actually MOVED within the last N minutes / M snapshots before permitting a fill. This directly measures tradeable-ness and is immune to holidays, half-days, AND DST/clock bugs in one stroke; it piggybacks on the same per-symbol freshness read. (Stopgap alternative if the durable gate slips: a US-equity-holiday/half-day calendar predicate folded into the window check.) Threshold to be set from evidence (measure the in-RTH `last`-move inter-gap distribution, mirroring the C3 freshness measurement). **HOME (CC + Langston consensus 2026-06-14): NEW dedicated batch P19-B6.6 — price-discovery-liveness fill gate. B7b is HARD-GATED on "B6.6 green" alongside B6.5** (B7b cannot enable active fills until both the crypto resurrection AND the liveness gate close clean). **TO FORMALIZE in PHASE_19_PLAN §1 board + §5 decision-log + §6 pre-flight (B7b gate).** | OPEN

**#237 OPEN 2026-06-14 (P19-B4a C7 / scope A4 — RTB Phase-4 `asset_class SET NOT NULL` DEFERRED to a post-activation real soak).** The RTB `rtb_signals.asset_class` migration ladder: Phase 1 (`ADD COLUMN NULL`) + Phase 3 (CHECK + index) shipped earlier; B4a was scoped to ship Phase-4 `ALTER COLUMN asset_class SET NOT NULL`. Langston decision-6 mandated the ordering **resolver-backed write (A1.5/C1) → 48h zero-null soak with a REAL generator → SET NOT NULL**, because flipping NOT NULL before the resolver-backed write would let a silently-wrong `crypto_spot`-on-xStock row pass (corrupting, worse than null). **C7 finding: the soak CANNOT run in B4a.** The ONLY writer of `rtb_signals` is the active orchestrator path (`queueSQESignal`, orchestrator:708); active-paper trading is DORMANT (the C2 xStock dispatch is gated off on `isEngineActive`, crypto active path off, VTS never touches RTB). So zero rows are written while dormant → a "zero-null for 48h" soak is **vacuously true**, and the scope itself flags that a vacuous soak must not gate the flip. The substantive A4 deliverable — the **resolver-backed RTB write spine (C1, shipped)** — IS in B4a; the NOT-NULL flip is the final hardening that needs real writes to soak against. **HOME: B7b pre-flight (post-first-real-RTB-writes).** Sequence at activation: turn active-paper on (B7b) → observe real `rtb_signals` writes carry a correct stamped `asset_class` (zero `[B79.0n.RTB][QUEUE_FALLBACK]`, zero null) for the soak window → THEN apply `SET NOT NULL`. **TO FORMALIZE in PHASE_19_PLAN §6 (B7b pre-flight gate).** Langston Step-4 ratify the deferral (consistent with his decision-6 ordering). | OPEN

**#298 OPEN 2026-06-15 (Kyle UI report — Open Simulated Trades Symbol column) — several rows show the ticker echoed instead of the real company/token NAME on the middle line; root cause = NAME DATA gap in BOTH sources, NOT a broken resolver.** The Symbol cell (`machine-learning.tsx` `OpenTradesTable` ~:635-645, twin `ClosedTradesTable` ~:978) renders symbol / `getAssetName(symbol, assetClass)` / asset-class badge. `getAssetName` (`shared/asset-names.ts:240`) routes by class: **crypto → static map `CRYPTO_NAMES`** (`asset-names.ts:53-208`, keyed base symbol); **xStock → DB `xstock_spot_universe.name`** via the `/api/xstocks/asset-names` overlay (`routes.ts:8150`, `universe-service.ts`). The resolver fail-loud-falls-back to the BARE TICKER on a miss (`asset-names.ts:252/267`), so 'blank' is really a ticker-echo. **Two data gaps (verified):** (a) CRYPTO — **19 of 144** `CRYPTO_NAMES` entries are self-referential (name==ticker → echo): BNB, XRP, GMX, EOS, BILL, CC, CHIP, COTI, PLAY, PTB, SNDK, STBL, TRIA, XAN, XMN, XNY, XPL, XYZ, ZBT. (b) xStock — **live DB query CONFIRMED** PALL/USD IS present (`is_delisted=f`) but `name='PALL'` (ticker, not the real company name); **0 rows are NULL/blank** (so the subagent's 'PALL missing' hypothesis was WRONG — it's name-equals-ticker, same pattern as crypto). The xStock universe-discovery name-fetch evidently stored the ticker for PALL — possible discovery name-resolution gap (how many other xStocks carry ticker-as-name needs a `name==base` sweep). **FIX (two parts):** (1) DATA backfill — fill real names where they exist (crypto map + xStock DB/overrides) + investigate why discovery stored the ticker for PALL; (2) STRUCTURAL — in `getAssetName`, when resolved name == base ticker, return `null` so the UI hides the redundant line (the JSX `&&` guard at :638 already hides null) instead of echoing the ticker. Cosmetic/display-only — NO trading impact. **HOME: a small UI/data-quality fix batch.** **★UPDATE 2026-06-15 — STRUCTURAL DISPLAY FIX SHIPPED** (commit `534d582ed`, deployed HTTP200): `getAssetName` now returns `null` on a ticker-echo (name==base) or a miss, so the UI hides the redundant middle line instead of echoing the ticker. Real distinct names still render; the echo/blank-looking lines are gone. **STILL OPEN — scoped + Langston-approved (APPROVE-W-COND 2026-06-15), SPLIT into two batches (Langston Q5):** **B-NAMES = crypto** (server-side last-resort CoinGecko name resolver, write-through `asset_names` overlay table, tier-1 disambiguation = reuse the already-pinned trade CoinGecko-id, negative-cache, fail-graceful) — scope `B_NAMES_SCOPE.md` §6. **B-NAMES.1 = xStock** (the bounded Backed-tokenized-equity universe — provider metadata + curated static map, NOT a flaky stock API; ROOT-CAUSE the discovery name-fetch that stored the ticker-echo 'PALL' — it should store NULL not the echo). **#298 stays OPEN until BOTH B-NAMES + B-NAMES.1 land.** ★SCHEDULED (Kyle directive 2026-06-15, §9.4 concrete home): **B-NAMES.1 (xStock) runs IMMEDIATELY AFTER B-NAMES (crypto)** — back-to-back, no other batch bundles the xStock-universe-discovery work, so doing them in sequence closes #298 in one sweep instead of letting the xStock half linger. Sequence: B-NAMES Step-2 pre-audit -> B-NAMES crypto build/ship -> B-NAMES.1 xStock build/ship -> #298 CLOSE; this 2-batch detour sits between Kyle-facing UI work and resuming the heavier Phase-19 D5 isolation thrust. NEXT = B-NAMES Step-2 pre-audit (resolve the CoinGecko endpoint + where symbol->id mapping lives + xStock universe size). **★★UPDATE 2026-06-15 — B-NAMES (crypto half) SHIPPED + live-verified** (commit `f0cee92e7`, CI `27547075378` all-4-green, bench 1942/1942, deployed + migration applied): new `asset_names` overlay table + server-side CoinGecko resolver (TIER-0 pinned-id for the 21 in `SYMBOL_TO_COINGECKO_ID` -> TIER-1 `/coins/list` + NAMED market-cap-gap disambiguation [`DISAMBIGUATION_DOMINANCE_MULTIPLE=5`x, `DISAMBIGUATION_MIN_MCAP_FLOOR_USD=$10M`, lone-candidate accept-on-identity] -> `/coins/markets` name) + write-through + negative-cache + TWO-WAY counter (ambiguous vs hard_miss) + 6h background sweep over `vts_open_trades` crypto symbols + `/api/crypto/asset-names` overlay endpoint + client overlay (map-first, overlay-second) + 10 unit tests. **First live sweep: 196 unresolved -> 184 names backfilled (181 tier-1 + 3 tier-0 pinned), 9 ambiguous-skip, 3 hard-miss, 0 errors.** §9.3 UI-verified: AR/USD (not in the curated map) now renders "Arweave" in the Open Simulated Trades Symbol column. **Crypto half DONE.** **★★UPDATE 2026-06-15 — B-NAMES.1 (xStock half) SHIPPED + live-verified → #298 RESOLVED (BOTH halves landed)** (commit `8e1526a9f`, CI `27556282043` all-4-green, bench 1945/1945, deployed + migration applied + backfill ran). ROOT-CAUSE fix (Langston C5): `XstockSpotEntry.name` made nullable + the discoverer stores `?? null` (not `?? symbol.split('/')[0]`) at both sites (`discoverer.ts:603` upsert + `:638` file-cache) — a null name makes `getXstockName` return null and the UI hide the line, never a ticker-echo; DB column `xstock_spot_universe.name` dropped NOT NULL; compile-driven probe confirmed zero other consumers assume non-null. CURATED MAP (Langston Q2 — vetted static map over a flaky stock API): `CURATED_XSTOCK_NAMES` (33 bounded Backed-ETF/foreign-equity symbols Finnhub's company-profile endpoint misses) wired into the discoverer fallback `override → Finnhub → curated → null`; no-echo invariant unit-tested; TBLL/TOTL web-verified. Backfill `scripts/b-names-1-xstock-name-backfill.ts` (imports the map = SSOT, idempotent `WHERE name==ticker`) updated all **33** existing ticker-echo rows. **§9.3 UI-verified: PALL now renders "abrdn Physical Palladium Shares ETF"** (Kyle's exact screenshot example), plus PPLT/SLV/SPY/SCHF/XBI/TQQQ; `/api/xstocks/asset-names` count=490 all-named. **Langston Step-4 CHANGES-NEEDED → fixed in-batch → APPROVE:** he caught that only the Stage-5 (DB upsert) fallback site had landed — the Stage-8 file-cache writer still recomputed `name ?? sym.split('/')[0]`, leaving the ticker-echo latent on the layer-3 cold-start fallback (the compile-probe couldn't catch it — `string` is assignable to `string|null`). FIXED with his "better still" option (commit `ce9b8b9a3`, CI `27556937761` all-4-green): Stage-5 captures each computed `finalEntry` into a Map, Stage-8 REUSES it (`Array.from(computedEntries.entries())`) — single source of name computation, the file cache can never diverge / re-echo. **Close-verify (Langston condition):** ran a discovery refresh, then scanned the live xStock name overlay → **ZERO exact bare-ticker echoes across all 490 names** (root-cause bug gone; lone case-insensitive match LYFT→"Lyft" is a real name the UI guard hides as ticker-redundant — acceptable). The on-disk layer-3 file cache itself is separately non-functional on staging (pre-existing `writeFileCache` EACCES since ≥06-12 — homed **#299**), so the file-cache echo is moot on this box + the code fix is correct for any environment where the cache IS writable. **#298 CLOSED — both the crypto (B-NAMES) and xStock (B-NAMES.1) halves are shipped + UI-verified.** | RESOLVED

**#299 OPEN 2026-06-15 (surfaced during B-NAMES.1 cache-verify — PRE-EXISTING infra gap, NOT a B-NAMES.1 bug) — the xStock universe layer-3 file-cache fallback is silently non-functional on staging.** While verifying Langston's B-NAMES.1 close condition ("zero `name==ticker` rows in the re-stamped file cache"), found the file `/var/lib/dawntrader/xstock-universe-cache.json` does not exist and `writeFileCache` has been **EACCES-failing daily since at least 2026-06-12** (`error.log`: `EACCES: permission denied, mkdir '/var/lib/dawntrader'` at every 06:00 UTC discovery + the B-NAMES.1 manual refresh). The `deploy` user cannot create `/var/lib/dawntrader`. Effect: the universe-service **layer-3 file-cache durability fallback is disabled on staging** — if the DB (layer-2) were unreachable at a cold start, boot would skip the (empty) layer-3 and fall to layer-4 bootstrap (degraded universe) instead of the last-known-good snapshot. NOT user-facing today (layer-2 DB is the live path + healthy) and NOT introduced by B-NAMES.1 (predates it) — but it means the B-NAMES.1 file-cache echo fix is unobservable on staging specifically (the file is never written), and the durability fallback the file cache exists to provide is absent. **FIX (low-effort, ops):** create `/var/lib/dawntrader` owned by `deploy` (`sudo mkdir -p /var/lib/dawntrader && chown deploy:deploy /var/lib/dawntrader`) OR set `XSTOCK_UNIVERSE_CACHE_PATH` in the deploy `.env` to a deploy-writable path (e.g. `/home/deploy/dawntrader/.cache/xstock-universe-cache.json`). After the fix, re-run a discovery refresh and confirm the file writes + carries zero `name==ticker` rows (the B-NAMES.1 verification that's currently vacuous on staging). **★FIXED + RESOLVED 2026-06-15 (same day — Kyle flagged that "next housekeeping pass" was too vague per §9.4):** applied `mkdir -p /var/lib/dawntrader && chown deploy:deploy /var/lib/dawntrader && chmod 755` on staging + **write-PROVEN** as the deploy user (`touch` succeeded — the exact operation that was EACCES-failing now works). The permission gap (the actual bug) is definitively closed; the layer-3 file cache now populates on the next discovery run (daily 06:00 UTC cron or a manual `/internal/universe-discovery/refresh`). Observable confirmation (deterministic downstream effect, 30-sec check): the file `/var/lib/dawntrader/xstock-universe-cache.json` exists + carries 0 `name==ticker` rows after the next discovery. NOTE — this is a host-level dir created outside the deploy script, so it does NOT auto-recreate on a fresh server rebuild; **if staging is ever re-provisioned, fold `mkdir+chown /var/lib/dawntrader` into the provisioning/deploy script (or set `XSTOCK_UNIVERSE_CACHE_PATH` to a path under `/home/deploy`).** Surfaced + fixed by CC 2026-06-15. | RESOLVED

**#297 OPEN 2026-06-15 (P19-B4b D5 verify-before-cut — audit correction) — `global.tradingEngines` is a NEVER-INITIALIZED vestigial global with 5 live referencing files; removal needs the dormant "live-engine / agent-intent" subsystem investigated first.** The D1 split-brain audit claimed `global.tradingEngines` was DEAD (zero non-test writers) and homed it for on-the-spot deletion in D5. **At D5 entry, direct grep falsified that:** it is referenced by `intent-executor.ts:233,239,281,463,476` (incl. unguarded `.set()` writers; the `intentExecutor` singleton is imported by `routes.ts:10825,10857`), `health-monitor.ts:444` (wired at boot via `index.ts:1434`), `context-refresh-coordinator.ts:198,304`, `state-awareness.ts:317`, `routes.ts:4059` (live-mode force-stop) — and is **NEVER assigned anywhere** (always `undefined`; most readers guard via `if(!x)`/`?.`, but `intent-executor.ts:239` + `routes.ts:4059` are unguarded latent-throws if those dormant paths run). So it is NOT a clean zero-writer deletion. **Disposition (rule 18 — schedule, don't cut):** (1) investigate whether the live-engine / agent-intent subsystem (`intent-executor`, `state-awareness`, `health-monitor`'s live-engine branch, `context-refresh-coordinator`, `server/agent/bridge/*`) is live or fully dormant; (2) if dormant, remove the global AND all five files' references together (full blast-radius, DELETED_COMPONENTS_LOG); if any is live, leave + document. **REMOVED from P19-B4b D5.** **HOME: a dedicated legacy-subsystem investigation (Phase-19 small-batch or Phase-16 sweep — decide at next touch); revisit trigger = any edit to intent-executor / state-awareness / the agent bridge.** Reinforces rule-18 "certainty-before-cutting" + rule-6 "never trust a compacted/second-hand claim — verify the file." **★ADDED 2026-06-15 (P19-B4b D5 R1 — Langston Step-4 condition): a deferred-correctness assumption now rides on this issue.** D5 routed `intent-executor.ts` + `state-awareness.ts`'s raw `global.globalPaperPortfolioManager` access through the new per-mode accessor with a HARDCODED `mode='paper'` (correct today because the subsystem is paper-only AND dormant). When THIS issue's investigation revives intent-executor's LIVE branch, that `'paper'` default at `executeStartTrading`/`executeStopTrading`/:497 + `state-awareness.getTradingStatus` becomes WRONG for the live path and MUST be revisited together with the `new PaperPortfolioManager(userId)` wrong-arg call (also left untouched in D5). Do not let the unwind inherit a silent wrong-mode default. | OPEN

**#296 OPEN 2026-06-15 (P19-B4b D1 audit / Langston Step-2 note) — consolidate the 35 ad-hoc `new KrakenService()` call-sites onto ONE shared rate-limited client. PARTIAL in D5; residual homed here.** The D1 split-brain audit found `new KrakenService()` constructed at **35 non-test sites across ~29 files**, each with its OWN per-instance `rateLimitStates` map — so a 120s `Temporary lockout` learned on one instance is invisible to the next (a correctness gap today, independent of mode), and there is no single rate-limit lane to isolate paper from live. **★UPDATE 2026-06-15 — S3 FULLY DEFERRED out of P19-B4b D5; this issue is now the SINGLE home for the whole Kraken-limiter consolidation (verify-before-edit + Langston Step-4 concur).** TWO findings at D5 implementation flipped the original plan: **(1) `kraken.ts` is a 🔒 LOCKED MODULE** (`Directive 8.8.4-A4.R10R-4` — "changes require a formal directive"); the rate-limit logic the shared limiter must replace lives INSIDE it, so S3 cannot be built without touching the locked module — CC did NOT touch it. **(2) The R2 key-dimension correction (Langston AGREE, C3 amended):** key the limiter by **CREDENTIAL IDENTITY (API-key-derived), NOT `${userId}:${mode}`** — paper+live share ONE Kraken API key today, so `EGeneral:Temporary lockout` is **account-wide and correctly shared**; mode-keying a shared-key account lockout would let the non-triggering mode keep hammering the locked account and DEEPEN the real lockout (worse than the current fragmentation). It's also forward-compatible: if paper later gets its own Kraken test-env credential (#227), the key differs and the limiters auto-separate — no mode logic. So S3 is **NOT a hard Phase-21 split-brain blocker** (per-instance maps today → no cross-mode corruption; a real account lockout SHOULD freeze both) — it is a **fragmentation/coordination** correctness fix. **DECISION (CC + Langston, Kyle-visible):** S3 ships as its own **follow-up batch carrying the formal locked-module directive** — build ONE shared module-level limiter keyed by credential identity (userId only as fallback) INSIDE the unlocked kraken.ts; because the limiter then lives in the class, **all 36 `new KrakenService()` instances share it automatically** (this SUBSUMES the original active-12-vs-residual-23 split — no per-site migration needed). **HOME: a dedicated "S3 credential-keyed Kraken limiter" batch (post-B4b, requires the kraken.ts locked-module directive); revisit trigger = the locked-module unlock OR next Kraken-client touch.** To formalize in PHASE_19_PLAN §5 (decision log). | OPEN (S3 deferred from D5; named follow-up home recorded)

**#295 OPEN 2026-06-15 (Kyle directive — P19-B4a design correction) — replace the xStock RTH liquid-fill-window (a time-of-day PROXY) with a direct book-depth-sufficiency fill gate that works 24/5. HOME = P19-B4b.1.** B4a-C3 shipped a `liquid-fill-window` gate that restricts xStock active FILLS to US regular hours (09:30–16:00 ET), as a proxy for "is the book liquid enough to fill realistically." It was introduced ONLY because B4a had no live order-book-depth feed to measure book volume directly (justified at the time by evidence that xStock price-discovery concentrates in RTH: 12–22% of price moves vs 2–7% off-RTH). **Kyle (2026-06-15): xStocks trade 24/5; if fills are gated on book order volume, the gate should BE book-depth-sufficiency, not a clock — a sufficiently deep book off-hours SHOULD fill, and a thin book during RTH should NOT.** Agreed on the merits: the clock proxy is wrong in BOTH directions (it wrongly blocks a fillable off-hours order and wrongly passes a thin RTH order). **RESOLUTION:** once B4b.1 builds the real order-book-depth feed + depth-warmth assertion + depth-walked slippage + partial-fill realism, the liquid-fill-window CLOCK gate is RETIRED and replaced by a book-depth-sufficiency gate (warm + deep enough for this size at acceptable slippage), evaluated 24/5. The OTHER two C3 gates stay (price-freshness + silent-stall watchdog — both correct, both 24/5). **No live impact today** — B4a's gate is DORMANT (active trading off) and B4b.1 lands before B7b activation, so the clock never governs a real fill. To formalize in PHASE_19_PLAN §5 (decision log) at B4b/B4b.1 close. **★RESOLVED 2026-06-16 (P19-B4b.1 SHIPPED, commit `b74526dc3`, CI `27586477175` all-4-green, deployed restart#393, migration applied + 8 `fill_depth_gate` rows seeded).** The RTH clock fill-gate is RETIRED from `active-dispatch.ts`; replaced by the 24/5 book-depth-sufficiency + warmth gate at the engine open seam (`_evaluateOpenDepthGate`, both classes, DB-resolved fail-closed). Crypto walks the live WS book (depth=10); xStock uses the existing top-of-book `askDepthUsd`. The clock predicate + its 2 `liquid_fill_window_*` keys are RETAINED (verify-before-cut: the silent-stall watchdog uses them — confirmed 2 keys live in staging DB). **Cross-ref #236/B6.6:** xStock's 24/5 fill-quality guard is split between B4b.1's depth-sufficiency gate AND B6.6's price-discovery-liveness gate; the B7b pre-flight (new gate-#13) requires BOTH per class. Langston Step-1/2/4 APPROVE. | RESOLVED

The dead-on-active-path inventory (live-vs-legacy trace, server/ only): **(a)** `realtime-paper-executor.ts` — instantiated, referenced ONLY by one diagnostic endpoint (`routes.ts ~:10939`); **(b)** `pre-execution-validator.ts` — referenced only by `/api/trading/validate`, `intent-executor.ts:449` (the #297 dormant live-engine subsystem), + `b72-warmup`; **(c)** the duplicate book-walk math in `slippage-fee-model.ts` (left behind by B4b.1's extract); **(d)** the SECOND, dormant WS `book` connection `market-data-ws.ts` → `market-data-coordinator.getLatestOrderBook` (feeds only the dead executor + diagnostics — a duplicate of the active adapter's book). **DISPOSITION (§9.4 concrete home; Langston Q-D ratified):** a dedicated cleanup batch **P19-B4b.2**, sequenced BEFORE B7b (PHASE_19_PLAN §1 row). B4b.2 cuts the THREE CLEAN ones (a, c, d) independently; (b) `pre-execution-validator` removal COORDINATES with the #297 investigation (its only non-diagnostic caller is #297's `intent-executor`) — leave it pending #297 if not resolved by B4b.2, so the sweep is not an open loop. The duplicate book-walk math (c) is logged in DELETED_COMPONENTS_LOG as "left intentionally — deleted with #300/B4b.2" (rule-18). To formalize in PHASE_19_PLAN §1 (B4b.2 row) + §5 (decision log). **★RESOLVED 2026-06-16 (P19-B4b.2 SHIPPED — code `977f3be08`, CI `27598725568` all-4-green, deployed restart#394, root HTTP200, `/api/execution/metrics` now 404, bench tsc no-regression + vitest 1979/1979; Langston Step-1/2/4 APPROVE).** Cut the cleanly-dead machinery: **(a)** `realtime-paper-executor.ts` DELETED (whole file → `_archive/deleted-code/`) — `executeTrade` had 0 callers, `recordPaperTrade` never finished; its 2 live `getStatus()` consumers handled (dead `/api/execution/metrics` endpoint removed; `system-health-monitor.getExecutionMetrics()` rerouted value-identically to mdCoordinator/rateControl/executionTiming — Langston-verified line-by-line). **(d)** dead order-book sub-path removed from `market-data-coordinator` (`latestOrderBooks` map + `orderbook` handler + `getLatestOrderBook`) + the dead `'orderbook'` emission from `market-data-ws` (the `book` subscription + the midpoint-tick emission KEPT — load-bearing for the live tick stream). **★Framing CORRECTION (the original #300 plan was inaccurate in 2 places):** (i) `realtime-paper-executor` was referenced by TWO live tendrils, not "only a diagnostic endpoint" (also `system-health-monitor` + a constructor tick-listener); (ii) the "dup 2nd WS book path" is NOT a clean module cut — `market-data-coordinator` + `market-data-ws` are LIVE shared infra (feed-integrity-monitor/health-monitor/parity-gate), only the order-book SUB-PATH was dead. **(c)** `calculatePriceImpact` KEPT as the depth-walk golden-test reference (parent module still imported by `pre-execution-validator` + `routes.ts`). **(b)** `pre-execution-validator` LEFT pending #297. **#303 OPEN 2026-06-17 (P19-B6 Step-4 / Langston ask E — §13 concrete home) — the daily-loss-budget kill switch counts REALIZED 24h P&L only; a large open-position UNREALIZED drawdown never trips the kill** (the evaluator fires on a trade CLOSE, and `calculate24hPL` sums realized closed-trade P&L over the session-anchored 24h window — no mark-to-market of open positions). Surfaced + homed by CC + Langston Step-4 review 2026-06-17. | OPEN

**#238 OPEN 2026-06-14 (P19-B4a C5 / Langston Step-4 ratification condition) — default-open active strategy gate needs a per-class `strategy_gates` seed + a B7b pre-flight non-empty assertion before activation.** C5 disposed the hardcoded `enabledStrategies` allowlist and replaced it with the DB-resolved `isStrategyEnabledForAssetClass` gate, which is DEFAULT-OPEN (no `strategy_gates` row for a `(strategy, asset_class)` → enabled). Langston ratified default-open for B4a (the active path is dormant; explicit-allowlist would black out all crypto until a seed migration; the resolver throwing on cold cache is the real fail-hard). BUT default-open has a real activation edge: a freshly-onboarded asset class with ZERO `strategy_gates` rows runs EVERY strategy by default — for a thin, less-tested class that is uncontrolled exposure if seeding is forgotten. CURRENT STATE: `xstock_spot` IS fully seeded (19 rows: 10 enabled / 9 disabled, from `2026-05-11-b79-0m-a-xstock-strategy-gates-seeds.sql`); `crypto_spot` has ZERO rows → default-open-everything at activation. **HOME (Langston Step-4): B7b activation pre-flight.** Two deliverables before B7b switches a class on: (a) a `strategy_gates` seed migration establishing the INTENDED per-class enablement for `crypto_spot` (and re-confirm `xstock_spot`); (b) the B7b pre-flight MUST assert `strategy_gates` is NON-EMPTY for any asset class being switched on — so default-open can never silently green-light the full strategy set on a thin class. **TO FORMALIZE in PHASE_19_PLAN §5 (decision log) + §6 (B7b pre-flight gate).** | OPEN

**#320 OPEN 2026-06-17 (P19-B6.5a Step-2 / Langston ruling Q-B — §13 concrete home) — RTB defense-in-depth: whether the ready-to-buy admission must reject a queued signal whose asset class is gate-INACTIVE, on top of the B6.5a entry gates.** B6.5a applies the new per-(mode,asset_class) active gate at the two class ENTRY points only (`fx5-scanner.ts:543-555` crypto scan, `xstock_spot/active-dispatch.ts:122-123` xStock dispatch) — an inactive class never emits a signal, so RTB (`ready_to_buy_service.ts:593-594, 786-787`) should never receive one. Langston ruled entry-only is acceptable **CONDITIONAL**: B6.5b's accretion-delta audit MUST explicitly trace every RTB admission path and PROVE no signal can reach the queue past the entry gates. **HOME (CONCRETE, Langston Q-B): a P19-B6.5b deliverable** — if the audit proves the entry gates are the sole admission path, entry-only stands (with an explanatory comment left at the RTB admission sites noting the upstream guarantee); if the audit finds ANY path that injects past the entries, RTB per-signal `assetClass`-active enforcement goes in **as part of B6.5b, not "later."** Surfaced + homed by CC + Langston Step-2 review 2026-06-17. | OPEN

**#321 OPEN 2026-06-17 (P19-B6.5a Step-4 / Langston Q1 — §13 concrete home) — `tradingStateSync.witnessAssetClassEmissionWhileInactive(mode,class)` is a defined-but-UNCALLED "hard witness" method.** B6.5a shipped it as the Q-D hard-breach observability hook (records a `LIVENESS_SPLIT` if a class emits active output while its gate is OFF), but it has **no call site** — by construction the entry gate prevents emission-while-OFF, so there is nowhere to wire it YET. Per §15 an uncalled method should not linger. Langston (Step-4) ruled it acceptable to ship ONLY because B6.5b is the exact place a real call site appears: the accretion-delta audit that hunts for an ALTERNATE emission path (one that bypasses the entry gates) is what would instrument the breach. **HOME (CONCRETE, Langston Q1): P19-B6.5b deliverable** — during B6.5b's audit, either (a) WIRE the method at the alternate emission path the audit identifies, OR (b) DELETE it if the audit proves the entry gates are the sole emission path. **★Also captured for B6.5b notes (Langston Q3): the dual-active edge** — `fx5-scanner` reads only the RESOLVED active mode's context for the crypto gate (fine today since one mode is active at a time; but if live+paper are ever BOTH ON, the crypto gate would consult only the live-resolved context). Record in the B6.5b audit so it is on file. Surfaced + homed by CC + Langston Step-4 review 2026-06-17. **★REFRAME (Kyle directive 2026-06-17): the dual-active case is NOT an edge case — it is the INTENDED STEADY STATE.** Once live launches, paper mode keeps running CONTINUOUSLY alongside live as the calibration/new-functionality testing ground (paper = "VTS that goes through the FULL pipeline" — SQE emits one best signal per cycle, not one per strategy). Both modes active simultaneously was the DRIVING FORCE behind the paper/live cleave (the between-Phase-24-and-19 work; B4b D5 isolation). So the `fx5-scanner` single-`activeMode`-per-tick scan loop AND this per-class gate's single-context read BOTH must handle concurrent paper+live — a **Phase-21 co-run precondition** (alongside the B4b D5 `LIVENESS_SPLIT` 0-during-co-run gate). **HOME re-pointed: Phase-21 live+paper co-run** (B6.5b is paper-ONLY so it only RECORDS this, does not fix it). | OPEN

**#322 OPEN 2026-06-17 (Kyle directive — live-mode-creation requirement; §9.4 home) — "test-in-paper / bypass-in-live" capability gating.** Live and paper will share the SAME pipeline except at the final order-placement seam (P19-B2 Option-A reuse-by-extension). Since paper is the permanent testing ground for new functionality + calibration while live runs real money, **new/experimental functionality must be runnable in PAPER but BYPASSABLE in LIVE** — a per-capability feature-gate keyed by mode (test in paper, skip in live until proven), so unproven logic never touches live orders. NOT a build now; a forward architectural requirement Kyle wants captured. **HOME (CONFIRMED, Kyle directive 2026-06-17): P19-B15** — a dedicated Phase-19 batch, scheduled LAST in the Phase-19 board (it pairs with no existing batch; Kyle: "give it a confirmed scheduled place in Phase 19 … if it doesn't fit with anything else, save it until last"). Design to be brought to Langston when B15 is scoped. Relates to #321 (both-modes steady state) + the per-class gate (B6.5a, the first such mode-keyed gate). | OPEN

Recorded so the consideration isn't lost. | OPEN

**#325 OPEN 2026-06-17 (P19-B6.5c gate-10 dry-run / §9.4 concrete home; BLOCKS gate-10 + B7b) — TCL→paper-execution-engine open-path silent failure: a sized crypto active-paper signal never opens a paper trade.** The B6.5c repair (the `cwqi` DB-drift DROP + the pattern→canonical-strategy resolve) cleared both ready-to-buy-insert breaks, so crypto signals now reach ready-to-buy with valid canonical names — but the FULL closed-trade lifecycle (B6.5c objective 5 / gate-10) STILL does not complete: a sized signal falls out somewhere in the TCL→paper-execution-engine handoff WITHOUT opening a position. The system's own open-path invariant monitor `[8.8.3-I3]` reports the diagnostic signature **attempts > 0, opened = 0, blocked = 0, reasonSum = 0** (observed attempts=11/opened=0/blocked=0/reasonSum=0 in the reverted dry-run) — i.e. the signal is counted as an open ATTEMPT but falls out BEFORE any block-reason or telemetry is recorded, which points at an un-awaited promise / a swallowed throw / an early-return-with-no-telemetry inside the TCL→PEE open path (this path has been untested since Phase 8). Surfaced by the P19-B6.5c gate-10 dry-run. **HOME (CONCRETE): dedicated batch `P19-B6.5e` — "TCL→paper-execution-engine open-path silent-failure repair + gate-10 closed-lifecycle home,"** which OWNS gate-10 / the ≥1 FULL closed-trade-lifecycle proof (open→exit→close→cooldown→telemetry); may promote to a full `P19-B7` if the open-path surface is large. **B7b stays HARD-GATED on the closed-lifecycle proof, now owned by B6.5e** (PHASE_19_PLAN §6 gate 10). Surfaced + homed by CC + Langston ruling 2026-06-17. **★RESOLVED 2026-06-18 (P19-B6.5e — the silent failure is fixed; the open path executes correctly).** ROOT CAUSE confirmed (Step-1.a): `executeSimulatedTrade` was `void`-returning with ~7 bare-`return` post-guardrail early-exits invisible to the I3 invariant, and `executePromotedSignal` inferred success by a trade-count delta — so any post-guardrail open failure produced "no new trade" with no error / no reason (the `reasonSum=0` signature). FIX: a typed `OpenOutcome` return + a NEW `openFailedTotal` / `openFailedByStage` third term in `rtb-metrics-service` (the I3 invariant is now `attempts === opened + blocked + openFailed`; recorded via `recordOpenFailed(symbol,strategy,stage,reason)`). PROVEN LIVE on the deployed code (`dbd0a2283`, CI `27731034448` all-4-green, staging restart#404): the contained crypto dry-run reconciled the invariant and NAMED the stage — `[8.8.3-I3][INVARIANT_CHECK][OK] attempts=8 opened=0 blocked=0 openFailed=8 [EV_REJECT:8]` — so a sized signal can never silently vanish again. **The open is NOT broken in execution:** it reaches the Net-Expectancy gate (11.8B), which HONESTLY rejects every crypto signal (NetEV<0 after friction) because the EV kernel runs on DEFAULT pWin=0.60/DI=50/VolNoise=0.300 inputs (the already-homed #233 — fixing the EV inputs, NOT the open path, is the lever for a real open). gate-10 (the ≥1 full closed crypto lifecycle proof) accordingly MOVED to the new batch P19-B6.5g (EV-input integrity + lifecycle proof); B7b stays hard-gated on B6.5g-green. Langston Step-1 PROCEED + Step-4 APPROVE (Phase A). | RESOLVED

**#326 OPEN 2026-06-17 (P19-B6.5c gate-10 dry-run / §9.4 concrete home — B63 DBS-propagation lineage; NON-FATAL) — B63 DBS-not-propagated hard-contract violation on some crypto pairs in the signal-orchestrator pattern-pool eval.** In the pattern-pool branch of the signal orchestrator, when a pair object lacks a `dbsScore`, `propagatedDbs` comes through undefined and the MCE throws the **B63 hard contract** (DBS-must-be-propagated). It is caught as a `[14.5][ORCHESTRATOR]` warn and those pairs are SKIPPED — so it is **non-fatal** (it does not crash the cycle), but it silently drops affected crypto pairs from the pattern-pool eval. Surfaced by the P19-B6.5c gate-10 dry-run; home confirmed via the B6.5c Step-8 exchange (Langston flagged "proposed≠confirmed", CC agreed standalone-item — converged, no Kyle escalation). | OPEN

**reorg-B2 surfaced items (2026-06-20, CC-B + Langston Step-4/Step-8 consensus on Discord) — concrete homes (§9.4):**
> **Issue-number deconfliction (2026-06-20):** these were originally homed #332/#333/#334 but #332/#333 COLLIDED with B-DISCORD's #332/#333 (the two concurrent CC sessions independently grabbed the same numbers — a shared-state hazard of the per-session split). Renumbered here: win-rate #332→**#335**, xStock-floor #333→**#336**; #334 (V4#2) kept (no collision); friction_safety_buffer = NEW **#337**.
- **#335 — Realized net-of-friction win-rate validation BEFORE crypto active-trading turns on (HOME: P19 reorg-B9 turn-on pre-flight gate).** At the Tier-1 taker fee wall (~1.8% round-trip) with pWin capped 0.60, the EV kernel is net-negative even at a 4% target / 1.6% stop (0.6·4 − 0.4·1.6 − 1.8 ≈ −0.04%), so the 11.8B EV gate honestly refuses to open crypto at taker. reorg-B2 ships the rung-1 PLUMBING; the EV gate is the safety. **Before B9 flips crypto active-paper ON, pull the realized net-of-friction win rate per regime and confirm it clears the breakeven (~58-63% at these values); if not, taker-rung-1 cannot open profitably.** The real opener = the maker build (reorg-B7, rung-2) + the pWin-ceiling recalibration (Phase-25). Decision-grade; matches the Phase-19 audit's fee-wall conclusion.
- **#336 — xStock per-class target floor must come DOWN (HOME: Phase-25 calibration item 25-17 ↔ this issue).** reorg-B2 seeds xStock target_floor_pct = 0.040 (= crypto, same account-wide fee wall) as a placeholder. A 4% intraday floor will under-trade xStock (equities don't move 4% intraday like alts). Phase-25 calibrates xStock's floor + reach_atr_max DOWN on its own realized data. NOT a verbal "Phase-25 nice-to-have" — a named Phase-25 calibration item (Langston #5).
- **#334 — reorg-B2 V4 literal #2: the pattern-path stop/target fallback (`signal-orchestrator.ts` `?? currentPrice * 0.97 / * 1.03`) (HOME: P19 pre-go-live cleanup batch).** V4 #1 (the `?? entry×1.015` target fallback in buildSizedSignalForStrategy) was deleted on the spot. #2 fabricates a sub-fee-wall stop/target when a pattern tradeSignal lacks geometry; removal is coupled to the sizing-requires-a-stop dependency (sizing runs before the normalizer's geometry-drop), so it gets a dated home rather than a risky in-batch removal: a pattern signal lacking geometry should DROP, not fabricate. Coordinate the removal with the sizing pre-check in the Phase-19 pre-go-live cleanup.
- **#337 — `expectancy_gates.friction_safety_buffer` per-class evaluation (HOME: Phase-25 calibration item 25-18 ↔ this issue; surfaced by Langston Step-8 2026-06-20).** reorg-B2 split the 6 ROI gate knobs per-class but kept `friction_safety_buffer` a single global `'*'` row BY DESIGN — it is a uniform safety **margin** on top of the friction **model**, and the model is already per-class (`fee_model` + per-class spreads carry the crypto-vs-xStock fee/spread/settlement differences), so a uniform margin on top is correct, not a missed split (CC-B + Langston consensus). **TRIGGER (explicit, §13):** once the per-class friction MODELS are calibrated in Phase-25, evaluate whether the buffer ITSELF should differ per-class (i.e. whether the calibrated per-class friction profiles leave a residual the uniform margin mis-sizes). Until then it stays global. Roadmap item carries this trigger verbatim. **OPEN (intentional-global; Phase-25 revisit).**
- **#338 — Signal-target-normalizer PLACEMENT: move the gates into the strategies' shared guard + DROP the floor-lift (HOME: NEW named P19 sub-batch `reorg-B2.1`; CC-B + Langston converged 2026-06-21, Kyle-requested review; PENDING Kyle go-ahead to schedule/build).** Kyle questioned whether reorg-B2's three normalizer ops belong in a post-hoc helper vs at signal generation. CC-B investigated all 19 strategies (`Langston Design Asks/REORG_B2_NORMALIZER_PLACEMENT_REVIEW_2026-06-21.md`); CC-B + Langston joint conclusion: **(a) DROP the floor-LIFT entirely** — it's a target MUTATION (split-brain: strategy's chosen target ≠ what executes) AND redundant with the **Net-Expectancy (11.8B) gate**, which already enforces beat-friction per-signal (RR×min-stop = 0.75% implied ≪ the 4% floor, so the floor was never doing reward/risk work — it was a coarse EV-gate proxy). **(b) CONSOLIDATE the RR gate to ONE per-class SSOT** in `strategy-helpers.applyGlobalGuards` — TODAY a live split-brain (helpers hardcode RR≥1.5, normalizer re-gates at per-class 2.5, stricter silently wins, the helper's guard becomes dead code misreporting the threshold — a NO-PATCHES violation in production). **(c) MOVE reachability** into the shared guard (formula-agnostic). **(d) WIRE the ~9 in-class detectors** (in `strategy-engine.ts`) into `applyGlobalGuards` (they bypass it today) → gates at signal generation + DRY + VTS auto-coverage (VTS calls `detect*` directly). **(e) SQE rejected as home** (downstream, also sees signal in isolation). **SEQUENCING (Langston, load-bearing): do NOT retire the normalizer first** — it is currently the ONLY RR/reachability coverage for the in-class strategies + VTS; it stays as the BRIDGE and retires LAST, once shared-guard coverage has moved. **TWO BLOCKING pre-pull gates before dropping the floor-lift (Langston):** (1) prove NO downstream consumer keys off the LIFTED target value (RTB pool, TEC sizing, diagnostics, VTS logging) — or migrate it in the same sub-batch; (2) confirm the NetEV gate genuinely fires on EVERY path the normalizer guarded (incl. any VTS-only/cold-start path) — else dropping the floor opens an ungated hole. **Filter-Diagnostics visibility** (Kyle's hard no-hidden-gates requirement, both VTS tabs) folds in here, sequenced to read from the shared guard (not built against the soon-retired helper). Also fixes the normalizer's wrong "21 strategies" comment → 19. **OPEN — Kyle GREENLIT 2026-06-21 (full workflow autonomously w/ Langston); scope `P19_REORG_B2_1_SCOPE.md` + pre-audit `P19_REORG_B2_1_PRE_AUDIT.md` committed, Langston Step-1 PROCEED, Step-2 in review. Both pre-pull gates verified clear (1a clean, 1b satisfied-w-nuance: active strict NetEV / VTS relaxed by design).**
- **#370 — Validate the Net-Expectancy gate's JUDGMENT QUALITY (does it actually select the trades that end up profitable?) — Kyle 2026-06-21 (HOME: Phase-25 calibration item 25-19 ↔ this issue).** reorg-B2.1 makes the Net-Expectancy gate the cost-coverage judge (drops the crude 4% floor). Kyle: "we have to look at its judgment skills — its ability to actually judge." The validation needs paper-active outcomes, so it's a Phase-25 item. **BASELINE = the VTS 4%-target study (`VTS_4PCT_TARGET_STUDY_2026-06-21.md`):** a flat 4% target does NOT predict crypto profitability (≥4% win 31% vs 33% for <4%), so the EV gate must do better than a crude proxy; xStock's 40-vs-26 win-rate split on target quality is a real (gross/confounded) signal the gate + calibration should capture. **Method (Phase-25):** measure whether NetEV-passing trades realize positive net-of-friction outcomes per regime/class vs NetEV-rejected; recalibrate the kernel inputs (pWin/DI/VolNoise — see #233) if its judgment is poor. ↔ POST_AUDIT_ROADMAP 25-19. **OPEN (Phase-25).**
- **#371 — reorg-B2.1 OBJ-5 guard-equivalence DELTA: measure GUARD-5 reachability over-rejection vs the retiring normalizer (HOME: reorg-B2.1 OBJ-5, the normalizer-retirement gate; CC-B + Langston Step-4 2026-06-21).** OBJ-2/3 added `validateReachability` (GUARD-5) to `applyGlobalGuards` using the strategy's `getEffectiveATR` (clamped) — while the normalizer's reachability uses `mceContext.indicators.atr`. Until the normalizer retires (OBJ-5), GUARD-5 is a NEW intersection gate running IN ADDITION to it, so the risk is **one-directional over-rejection** where `effectiveATR < mceContext.atr` makes `atrsToTarget` larger → a signal GUARD-5 drops that the normalizer would have passed. **OBJ-5 precondition (Langston): MEASURE this delta on staging (the two ATR sources + the RR net-neutrality are the two load-bearing identities of the equivalence proof) — not assert near-equivalence — before retiring the normalizer.** If the delta is material, reconcile the ATR source (use the same ATR in both, or accept the guard's effectiveATR as canonical with evidence). **SCOPE (widened OBJ-4b 2026-06-21): this divergence covers ALL guard-wired in-class strategies — including the 3 non-ATR-geometry ones (sma_trend_ride, vwap_bounce, dhma) which feed the reachability a `computeATR(priceHistory)` pair-ATR (their geometry is SMA/VWAP/realizedVol-based; reachability is a path-invariant PAIR property — Langston) — NOT only the ATR-native strategies. Same divergence class (guard-clamped ATR vs normalizer `mceContext.atr`); one measurement at OBJ-5 covers all of them, no second ticket.** **OBJ-5 IMPLEMENTATION NOTE (Langston Step-4 §13): `recordGuardEval` captures rr/pass/dropReason but NOT the `effectiveATR`/`atrsToTarget` values — so the OBJ-5 guard-vs-normalizer ATR correlation MUST add `effectiveATR` (+ `atrsToTarget`) capture to the tracker (and the normalizer-side `mceContext.atr` capture) before the divergence can be measured. Written here so it's not lost.** **OPEN (reorg-B2.1 OBJ-5).**
- **#372 — Per-strategy minRR calibration: the reorg-B2 `min_rr=2.5` seed SUPPRESSES most of the strategy suite (HOME: Phase-25, pairs with 25-2/3/10 / #336; INSTRUMENTATION PRECURSOR in reorg-B2.1 OBJ-4; CC-B + Langston 2026-06-21).** Per-strategy analysis (`Scope Files/REORG_B2_1_OBJ4_PER_STRATEGY_ANALYSIS_2026-06-21.md`): the per-class `min_rr=2.5` is higher than most strategies' DESIGNED reward-to-risk, so the normalizer drops them today (VTS).
